-bisimulation.

\dashv

Lemmas
4
,
5
,
16
give the
2ExpTime
upper bound for deciding
\mathsf{GML\!^{i,u}}
/
\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}
-Craig separation in Theorem
10
.
We obtain the
2ExpTime
upper bound for
\mathsf{GML\!^{i,u}}/\mathsf{ML\!^{i}}
by dropping the condition that
M_{1}
and
M_{2}
in
M=(M_{1},M_{2})
are both non-empty. The one for
\mathsf{GML\!^{i,u}}/\mathsf{ML\!^{u}}
is obtained by treating the inverse
R^{-}
with
R\in\varrho
in the same way as
R\in\sigma\setminus\varrho
. Both of these modifications give the upper bound for
\mathsf{GML\!^{i,u}}/\mathsf{ML}
.

It remains to establish the
coNExpTime
upper bounds for
\mathsf{GML\!^{i}}/\mathsf{ML\!^{i}}
and
\mathsf{GML\!^{i}}/\mathsf{ML}
.
Suppose
\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2}
are
\mathsf{GML\!^{i}}
-formulas.
We again ignore everything related to
\Diamondblack
and admit mosaics
M=(M_{1},M_{2})
with
M_{1}=\emptyset
or
M_{2}=\emptyset
(but not both). Let
m=\max\{\textit{md}(\varphi_{1}),\textit{md}(\varphi_{2})\}
. Then it follows from the proof of Lemma
16
and the definition of
\boldsymbol{\beta}
in it that there exist
\mathfrak{A}_{i},a_{i}
,
i=1,2
, with
\mathfrak{A}_{i}\models\varphi_{i}(a_{i})
and
\mathfrak{A}_{1},a_{1}\sim_{\mathsf{ML\!^{i}}(\varrho)}\mathfrak{A}_{2},a_{2}
iff there are words of the form

s=M_{0}M_{1}\dots M_{n}
with
n\leq\textit{md}(\varphi,\psi)
and
M_{j}=(M_{j,1},M_{j,2})
such that

–

M_{j}
is a mosaic of size
\leq k_{\varphi_{1}\varphi_{2}}^{2}
, for
j\leq n
;

–

M_{0}=M^{\ast}
, for a fixed root mosaic
M^{*}=(M_{1}^{\ast},M_{2}^{\ast})
such that there are
(\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{s}_{p},\mathfrak{s}_{c})\in M_{1}^{\ast}
and
(\mathfrak{t}^{\prime},\mathfrak{s}_{p}^{\prime},\mathfrak{s}_{c}^{\prime})\in M_{2}^{*}
with
\varphi_{1}\in\mathfrak{t}
and
\varphi_{2}\in\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}
;

–

M_{j+1}\in\mathcal{M}_{M_{j}}
, for a witness
\mathcal{M}_{M_{j}}
for
M_{j}
with
|\mathcal{M}_{M_{j}}|\leq k_{\varphi_{1}\varphi_{2}}
and all
j<n
.

A
NExpTime
algorithm deciding non-separation could guess words of the form
s=M_{0}M_{1}\dots M_{n}
and then check in exponential time that they satisfy the three conditions above.

The matching lower bounds are proved as in the previous section. This completes the proof of Theorem
10
.

VII
Definability

In this section, we show that, for all pairs
L/L^{\prime}
of logics in Table
I
, the
L/L^{\prime}
-definability problem can be reduced in polynomial time to validity in
L
. The complexity results for definability in Table
I
follow then from the known complexities of
L
-validity. In the proof, given any
L
-formula
\varphi
, we construct an
L^{\prime}
-formula
\mathit{fl}(\varphi)
—mostly of polynomial DAG-size—such that
\mathit{fl}(\varphi)
is a definition of
\varphi
iff
\varphi
is
L^{\prime}
-definable.
In the cases of
\mathsf{C^{2}}/L^{\prime}
-definability with
L^{\prime}\in\{\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}},\mathsf{ML\!^{i}},\mathsf{ML}\}
,
equivalent polynomial DAG-size formulas do not always exist. We nevertheless obtain polytime reductions to
\mathsf{C^{2}}
-validity by
working with a succinct version of
\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}
.

Let
\mathfrak{A}=({\sf dom}(\mathfrak{A}),(R^{\mathfrak{A}})_{R\in\sigma},(c^{\mathfrak{A}})_{c\in\sigma})
be a
\sigma
-structure and let
\mathbb{N}^{\infty}=\{1,2,\dots\}\cup\{\omega\}
with
k<\omega
, for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
. For
\kappa\in\mathbb{N}^{\infty}
, the
\kappa
-expansion

\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}
of
\mathfrak{A}
is defined by taking

–

{\sf dom}(\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa})=\bigcup_{a\in{\sf dom}(\mathfrak{A})}\bar{a}
, where

\bar{a}=\{(a,0)\}
if
a=c^{\mathfrak{A}}
, for some constant
c
, and
\bar{a}=\{(a,i)\mid 0\leq i<\kappa\}
otherwise;

–

A^{\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}}=\bigcup_{a\in A^{\mathfrak{A}}}\bar{a}
, for a unary predicate
A\in\sigma
;

–

R^{\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}}=\bigcup_{(a,b)\in R^{\mathfrak{A}}}\bar{a}\times\bar{b}
, for a binary predicate
R\in\sigma
;

–

c^{\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}}=(c^{\mathfrak{A}},0)
, for a constant
c\in\sigma
.

In other words,
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}
creates
\kappa
-many copies of each element of
{\sf dom}(\mathfrak{A})
except for those that interpret constants in
\sigma
, in which case we only keep a single copy. The next lemma follows directly from the definition by a straightforward induction:

Lemma 17
.

If
L
is equality-free
\mathsf{FO}
or a fragment of
\mathsf{ML\!^{i,n,u}}
, then, for any pointed
\sigma
-structure
\mathfrak{A},a
and any
i<\kappa
, we have
\mathfrak{A},a\equiv_{L(\sigma)}\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa},(a,i)
.

We remind the reader that, in this paper,
\sigma
in
L(\sigma)
may contain constants only if
L
is a modal logic with nominals. We first consider the case when
L
ranges over equality-free
\mathsf{C^{2}}
and nominal-free modal logics.
The
flattening

\mathit{fl}(\varphi)
of a formula
\varphi
in equality-free
\mathsf{C^{2}}
is defined by replacing every
\exists^{\geq k}x
with
\exists x
; similarly, the
flattening

\mathit{fl}(\varphi)
of a
\mathsf{GML\!^{i,u}}
-formula
\varphi

is defined by replacing
every
\Diamond^{\geq k}_{R}
in
\varphi
with
\Diamond_{R}
.
As before, we denote by
k^{\bullet}
the maximal number
k
such that
\exists^{\geq k}x
or, respectively, some
\Diamond_{R}^{\geq k}
occurs in the given formula
\varphi
. The next observation follows directly from the definitions:

Lemma 18
.

Suppose that
\sigma
is constant-free,
\varphi
is a
\mathsf{GML\!^{i,u}}(\sigma)
-formula or
\varphi(x)
is an equality-free
\mathsf{C^{2}}(\sigma)
-formula, and that
k^{\bullet}<\kappa<\kappa^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}^{\infty}
. Then
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}\models\varphi(a,i)
iff
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}\models\mathit{fl}(\varphi)(a,i)

iff
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa^{\prime}}\models\mathit{fl}(\varphi)(a,i)
iff
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa^{\prime}}\models\varphi(a,i)
, for all pointed
\sigma
-structures
\mathfrak{A},a
and all
i<\kappa
.

We can now show that
\mathit{fl}(\varphi)
is an
L^{\prime}
-formula defining
\varphi
whenever such an
L^{\prime}
-definition exists.

Theorem 19
.

Let
L/L^{\prime}
be either
\mathsf{C^{2}}/\mathsf{FO^{2}}
with equality-free
\mathsf{C^{2}}
and
\mathsf{FO^{2}}
, or any pair of modal nominal-free logics from Table
I
. Then an
L
-formula
\varphi
is
L^{\prime}
-definable iff
\models\varphi\leftrightarrow\mathit{fl}(\varphi)
.

Proof.

(\Rightarrow)
Suppose
\varphi
is definable by an
L^{\prime}
-formula
\varphi^{\prime}
and
\sigma=\mathit{sig}(\varphi)\cup\mathit{sig}(\varphi^{\prime})
. By definition,
\sigma
contains no constants. Take any pointed
\sigma
-structure
\mathfrak{A},a
. By the choice of
\varphi^{\prime}
and Lemmas
17
and
18
,
\mathfrak{A}\models\varphi(a)
iff
\mathfrak{A}\models\varphi^{\prime}(a)
iff
\mathfrak{A}^{\omega}\models\varphi^{\prime}(a,0)
iff
\mathfrak{A}^{\omega}\models\varphi(a,0)
iff
\mathfrak{A}^{\omega}\models\mathit{fl}(\varphi)(a,0)
iff
\mathfrak{A}\models\mathit{fl}(\varphi)(a)
, which yields
\models\varphi\leftrightarrow\mathit{fl}(\varphi)
. The implication
(\Leftarrow)
is trivial.

\dashv

By using the criterion of Theorem
19
, one can readily verify the claims about definability stated in Example
1
.

As
\mathit{fl}(\varphi)
is constructed in polytime, we obtain:

Corollary 20
.

Let
L/L^{\prime}
be either
\mathsf{C^{2}}/\mathsf{FO^{2}}
without equality or any pair of modal nominal-free logics from Table
I
.
Then
L/L^{\prime}
-definability is polynomial-time reducible to
L
-validity.

We apply a similar technique to the modal logics with nominals, which requires a slightly more complicated transformation than merely replacing
k
in
\Diamond\!^{\geq k}_{R}
by
1
. The reason is the definition of
\bar{a}
in
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}
, for
a=c^{\mathfrak{A}}
with
c\in\sigma
, where
a
does not have multiple copies unlike the non-constant elements.

Let
\varphi
be a
\mathsf{GML\!^{i,n,u}}(\sigma)
-formula. Denote by
\sigma_{c}
the set of constants in
\sigma
, assuming without loss of generality that
\sigma_{c}
is finite. Define the (
nominal aware
)
flattening

\mathit{fl}_{\sigma_{c}}(\varphi)
of
\varphi
in the same way as
\mathit{fl}(\varphi)
except that

\displaystyle\mathit{fl}_{\sigma_{c}}(\Diamond\!^{\geq k}_{R}\varphi)=\Diamond_{R}\big(\mathit{fl}_{\sigma_{c}}(\varphi)\land\bigwedge_{c\in\sigma_{c}}\neg N_{c}\big)\vee{}

\displaystyle\hskip 48.36958pt\bigvee_{\varrho\subseteq\sigma_{c},|\varrho|=k}\ \bigwedge_{c\in\varrho}\Diamond_{R}\big(\mathit{fl}_{\sigma_{c}}(\varphi)\land N_{c}\wedge\hskip-5.69054pt\bigwedge_{d\in\varrho\setminus\{c\}}\hskip-5.69054pt\neg N_{d}\big).

It is straightforward to lift Lemma
18
and
Theorem
19
to pairs
L/L^{\prime}
from Table
I
with
L
admitting nominals and the function
\mathit{fl}_{\sigma_{c}}(\varphi)
. In this case, we do not immediately get a polytime reduction of definability to validity since
\mathit{fl}_{\sigma_{c}}(\Diamond\!^{\geq k}_{R}\varphi)
contains exponentially-many disjuncts and
because
\mathit{fl}_{\sigma_{c}}(\varphi)
occurs more than once in
\mathit{fl}_{\sigma_{c}}(\Diamond\!^{\geq k}_{R}\varphi)
. A polytime reduction is obtained, however, by replacing the second disjunct of
\mathit{fl}_{\sigma_{c}}(\Diamond\!^{\geq k}_{R}\varphi)
by the equivalent
L
-formula

\Diamond_{R}^{\geq k}\big(\mathit{fl}_{\sigma_{c}}(\varphi)\wedge\bigvee_{c\in\sigma_{c}}N_{c}\big)
and by working with a DAG-representation.

Corollary 21
.

Let
L/L^{\prime}
be any pair of modal logics from Table
I
such that
L
admits nominals. Then
L/L^{\prime}
-definability is polytime reducible to
L
-validity.

We next consider the pair
\mathsf{C^{2}}/\mathsf{FO^{2}_{\neq}}
. Observe that the flattening defined above does not work now even if we admit equality in both logics. For example,
\varphi=\exists^{\geq 2}y\,\big(x=y\wedge A(y)\big)
is unsatisfiable (and so equivalent to an
\mathsf{FO^{2}_{\neq}}
-formula) but it is not equivalent to
\exists y\,\big(x=y\wedge A(y)\big)
.
To define
\mathit{fl}(\exists^{\geq k}x\,\varphi)
in this case so that Lemma
18
holds, we distinguish between
k=1
and
k\geq 2
, decomposing
\exists^{\geq k}x\,\psi
into

\exists^{\geq k}x\,\big[\big((x=y)\wedge\psi\big)\vee\big((x\not=y)\wedge\psi\big)\big],

where
\{x,y\}=\textit{var}
.
For
k=1
, we rewrite the disjuncts separately into formulas without equality, and for
k=2
, we only take a flattening of the second disjunct without equality; details are given in the appendix. One can then again lift Theorem
19
and obtain a polytime
reduction of
\mathsf{C^{2}}/\mathsf{FO^{2}_{\neq}}
-definability to
\mathsf{C^{2}}
-validity.
We note that the decidability status of
\mathsf{C^{2}}/\mathsf{FO^{2}}
-definability remains open.

In contrast to separation, the upper bounds for definability shown above cannot be easily extended from
L/L^{\prime}
to
L/L^{\prime\prime}
, where
\mathsf{ML}\subseteq L^{\prime\prime}\subseteq L^{\prime}
.
A fundamental difference from the pairs of logics considered above is that equivalent formulas in the weaker logic are sometimes exponentially larger even in DAG-size as demonstrated by the following:

Lemma 22
.

The formulas

\varphi_{n}(x)=\exists y\,\big[R(x,y)\wedge\bigwedge_{1\leq i\leq n}\big(A_{i}(x)\leftrightarrow A_{i}(y)\big)\big],\ \ n<\omega,

are
\mathsf{ML}
-definable, but any equivalent
\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}
-formulas are of exponential DAG-size.

Proof.

Suppose otherwise, that is,
\chi_{n}\in\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}
is of polynomial DAG-size
and
\mathfrak{A}\models\varphi_{n}(a)
iff
\mathfrak{A}\models\chi_{n}(a)
holds for all
\mathfrak{A},a
.
Consider the model
\mathfrak{A}_{n}=(W_{n},R^{\mathfrak{A}_{n}},A_{0}^{\mathfrak{A}_{n}},\ldots,A_{n-1}^{\mathfrak{A}_{n}})
with domain
W_{n}=\{w\}\cup X_{n}\cup X_{n}^{\prime}
, where

X_{n}=\{0,\ldots,2^{n}-1\}
and
X_{n}^{\prime}=\{0,\ldots,2^{n}-1\}\times\{1\}
, and

–

R^{\mathfrak{A}_{n}}=(\{w\}\times X_{n})\cup(X_{n}\times X_{n}^{\prime})
,

–

A_{i}^{\mathfrak{A}_{n}}
is such that the nodes
k\in X_{n}
and
(k,1)\in X_{n}^{\prime}
satisfy
k
encoded in binary using
A_{0},\ldots,A_{n-1}
.

Clearly,
\mathfrak{A}_{n}\models\Box_{R}\varphi_{n}(w)
, and so
\mathfrak{A}_{n}\models\Box_{R}\chi_{n}(w)
. Then, for every
\Diamond_{R}\chi\in\text{sub}(\chi_{n})
such that
\mathfrak{A}_{n}\models\Diamond_{R}\chi(v)
for some
v\in X_{n}
, we can select from
X_{n}^{\prime}
a
v_{\chi}\in X_{n}^{\prime}
with
\mathfrak{A}_{n}\models\chi(v_{\chi})
. Denote the set of selected nodes by
X_{n}^{\prime\prime}
.
Then the restriction of
\mathfrak{A}_{n}
to the set
\{w\}\cup X_{n}\cup X_{n}^{\prime\prime}
satisfies
\Box_{R}\chi
and
X_{n}^{\prime\prime}\subsetneq X_{n}^{\prime}
for sufficiently large
n
as the size of
X_{n}^{\prime\prime}
is bounded by the number of subformulas of
\chi_{n}
. By the semantics of
\Box_{R}\varphi_{n}
, we clearly have

\mathfrak{A}_{n}\not\models\Box_{R}\varphi_{n}(w)
for all
n>0
, which is a contradiction.

\dashv

We now introduce a more succinct version of
\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}
, which is included in the two-variable guarded fragment of first-order logic (and so satisfiability is still
ExpTime
-complete). Fix variables
x,y
. Then the formulas of suc
\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}
,
succinct
\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}
, are constructed from atoms
A(z)
and
\top(z)
with
z\in\{x,y\}

by taking the closure under the following rules:

1.

if
\varphi,\psi\in\text{suc}\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}
, then
\neg\varphi,\varphi\wedge\psi\in\text{suc}\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}
;

2.

if
\chi(x,y)\in\text{suc}\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}
and
x,y
are both free in
\chi(x,y)
, then
\exists y\,(R(x,y)\wedge\chi(x,y))\in\text{suc}\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}
,
\exists x\,(R(y,x)\wedge\chi(x,y))\in\text{suc}\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}
;

3.

if
\chi(x,y)\in\text{suc}\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}
and
x,y
are both free in
\chi(x,y)
, then
\exists y\,(R(y,x)\wedge\chi(x,y))\in\text{suc}\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}
,
\exists x\,(R(x,y)\wedge\chi(x,y))\in\text{suc}\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}
;

4.

if
\chi(x,y)\in\text{suc}\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}
and
x,y
are both free in
\chi(x,y)
, then

\exists y\,\chi(x,y)\in\text{suc}\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}
and
\exists x\,\chi(x,y)\in\text{suc}\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}
.

The languages suc
\mathsf{ML\!^{i}}
and suc
\mathsf{ML\!^{u}}
are obtained by dropping items 4) and 3), respectively. The language suc
\mathsf{ML}
is defined by dropping 3) and 4).

Lemma 23
.

Let
L\in\{\mathsf{ML},\mathsf{ML\!^{i}},\mathsf{ML\!^{u}},\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}\}
. Then suc
L
and
L
have the same expressive power in the sense that

(i)

for every
\varphi\in L
, one can construct in polynomial time a formula
\varphi^{\prime}(x)\in\text{suc}L

such that
\mathfrak{A}\models\varphi(a)
iff
\mathfrak{A}\models\varphi^{\prime}(a)
for all pointed
\mathfrak{A},a
;

(ii)

for every
\varphi(x)\in\text{suc}L
, one can construct in exponential time a formula
\varphi^{\prime}\in L

such that
\mathfrak{A}\models\varphi(a)
iff
\mathfrak{A}\models\varphi^{\prime}(a)
for all pointed
\mathfrak{A},a
.

Now, for every formula
\varphi(x)\in\mathsf{FO^{2}_{\neq}}
with exactly one free variable
x
, we define inductively the
modal reduct

m(\varphi(x))
in suc
\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}

by taking
m(A(x))=A(x)
,
m(\neg\varphi)=\neg m(\varphi)
,
m(\varphi\wedge\psi)=m(\varphi)\wedge m(\psi)
, and by defining
m(\exists x\,\psi)
as follows. Suppose
\psi
is a Boolean combination
\beta
of the form

\psi=\beta\big(\rho_{1},\ldots,\rho_{k_{1}},\gamma_{1}(x),\dots,\gamma_{k_{2}}(x),\xi_{1}(y),\dots,\xi_{k_{3}}(y)\big),

where

–

the
\rho_{i}
are binary atoms
R(x,y)
,
R(y,x)
;

–

the
\gamma_{i}(x)
are unary atoms of the form
A(x)
, or
R(x,x)
, or a formula of the form
\exists y\,\gamma_{i}^{\prime}
;

–

the
\xi_{i}(y)
are unary atoms of the form
A(y)
, or
R(y,y)
, or a formula of the form
\exists x\,\xi_{i}^{\prime}
.

We may assume that
x
and
y
are free in
\psi
. For
R\in\mathit{sig}(\varphi)
, we construct from
\psi
the formulas

–

\psi_{R(x,y)}
by replacing all
\rho_{i}
of the form
S(x,y)
and
S(y,x)
, for
S\not=R
, or of the form
R(y,x)
with
\neg\top(x)
, all
\gamma_{i}
of the form
S(x,x)
with
\neg\top(x)
, all
\xi_{i}
of the form
S(y,y)
with
\neg\top(y)
, and all
\rho_{i}
of the form
R(x,y)
with
\top(x)
;

–

\psi_{R(y,x)}
by replacing all
\rho_{i}
of the form
S(x,y)
and
S(y,x)
, for
S\not=R
, or of the form
R(x,y)
with
\neg\top(x)
, all
\gamma_{i}
of the form
S(x,x)
with
\neg\top(x)
, all
\xi_{i}
of the form
S(y,y)
with
\neg\top(y)
, and replace all
\rho_{i}
of the form
R(y,x)
with
\top(x)
;

–

\psi_{N}
by replacing all
\rho_{i}
of the form
S(x,y)
and
S(y,x)
and all

\gamma_{i}
of the form
S(x,x)
and all
\xi_{i}
of the form
S(y,y)
with
\neg\top(x)
.

Next, consider the formula

\psi_{1}=\bigvee_{R\in\mathit{sig}(\varphi)}\big(R(x,y)\wedge\psi\big)\vee\bigvee_{R\in\mathit{sig}(\varphi)}\big(R(y,x)\wedge\psi\big)\vee{}\\
\big[\psi\wedge\bigwedge_{R\in\mathit{sig}(\varphi)}\big(\neg R(x,y)\wedge\neg R(y,x)\big)\big],

which is equivalent to
\psi
. Then we obtain
m(\exists x\,\psi)
from

\exists x\,\psi_{1}
by setting:

m(\exists x\,\psi)=\bigvee_{R\in\mathit{sig}(\varphi)}\exists x\,\big(R(x,y)\wedge m(\psi_{R(x,y)})\big)\vee{}\\
\bigvee_{R\in\mathit{sig}(\varphi)}\exists x\,\big(R(y,x)\wedge m(\psi_{R(y,x)})\big)\vee\exists x\,m(\psi_{N}).

Clearly,
m(\varphi)
is in suc
\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}
and of polynomial DAG-size in the size of
\varphi
. We sketch how to prove that
\varphi(x)
is suc
\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}
-definable iff
\models\varphi\leftrightarrow m(\varphi)
:
given any structure
\mathfrak{A}
, one
takes its
twin-unfolding

\mathfrak{A}^{\ast}
that consists of the standard unfoldings of
\mathfrak{A}
along binary relations and their inverses, with at least two unrelated copies of any node. The structures
\mathfrak{A}
and
\mathfrak{A}^{\ast}
cannot be distinguished in suc
\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}
. In fact, one can prove that
\mathfrak{A}^{\ast}\models\varphi(a)
iff
\mathfrak{A}^{\ast}\models m(\varphi)(a)
, for all pointed
\mathfrak{A},a
. Then one can prove analogues of Lemmas
17
and
18
to show that
\varphi(x)
is suc
\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}
-definable iff
\models\varphi\leftrightarrow m(\varphi(x))
. Overall, this shows that
\mathsf{C^{2}}/\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}
definability is polytime reducible to
\mathsf{C^{2}}
-validity.

For
\text{suc}\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}/\text{suc}\mathsf{ML\!^{i}}
- and
\text{suc}\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}/\text{suc}\mathsf{ML}
-definability, we
say that
\mathfrak{A}_{0}
is a
generated substructure
of
\mathfrak{A}_{1}
if it is a substructure of
\mathfrak{A}_{1}
and whenever
a\in\text{dom}(\mathfrak{A}_{0})
and
(a,a^{\prime})\in R^{\mathfrak{A}_{1}}
or

(a^{\prime},a)\in R^{\mathfrak{A}_{1}}
, then
a^{\prime}\in{\sf dom}(\mathfrak{A}_{0})
. We call

\mathfrak{A}_{0}
a
forward generated substructure
of
\mathfrak{A}_{1}
if it is a substructure of
\mathfrak{A}_{1}
and whenever
a\in\text{dom}(\mathfrak{A}_{0})
and
(a,a^{\prime})\in R^{\mathfrak{A}_{1}}
, then
a^{\prime}\in{\sf dom}(\mathfrak{A}_{0})
.
We say that
\varphi
is
invariant under
(
forward
)
generated substructures
if, for all pointed
\mathfrak{A},a
and all (forward) generated substructures
\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}
of
\mathfrak{A}
with
a\in{\sf dom}(\mathfrak{A}^{\prime})
, we have
\mathfrak{A}\models\varphi(a)
iff
\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}\models\varphi^{\prime}(a)
.
We then show that

1.

\varphi\in\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}
is
\mathsf{ML\!^{i}}
-definable iff
\varphi
is invariant under generated substructures;

2.

\varphi\in\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}
is
\mathsf{ML}
-definable iff
\varphi
is invariant under forward generated substructures.

By encoding the problem of deciding whether a formula in
\text{suc}\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}

is invariant under (forward) generated substructures as a validity problem in

\text{suc}\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}
we obtain the following overall result.

Theorem 24
.

Let
L^{\prime}\in\{\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}},\mathsf{ML\!^{i}},\mathsf{ML}\}
. Then
\mathsf{C^{2}}/L^{\prime}
-definability is polytime reducible to
\mathsf{C^{2}}
-validity.

VIII
Uniform Separation

Let
L,L^{\prime}
be a pair of logics,
\sigma
a signature,
and
\varphi
an
L(\sigma)
-formula. An
L^{\prime}(\sigma)
-formula
\varphi^{\prime}
is called an
L^{\prime}(\sigma)
-uniform separator for
\varphi

if it is a logically strongest
L^{\prime}(\sigma)
-formula that follows from
\varphi
in the sense that
\varphi\models\varphi^{\prime}\models\chi
, for every
L^{\prime}(\sigma)
-formula
\chi
with
\varphi\models\chi
. The
uniform
L/L^{\prime}(\sigma)
-separation problem
is to decide, given an
L(\sigma)
-formula
\varphi
, whether it has an
L^{\prime}(\sigma)
-uniform separator. If such a separator for
\varphi
exists, it is unique up to logical equivalence and separates any
L^{\prime}(\sigma)
-separable
L(\sigma)
-formulas
\varphi
and
\psi
. Note, however, that uniform
L^{\prime}(\sigma)
-separators for distinct
\sigma
can be non-equivalent and that a uniform
L(\sigma)
-separator might only exist for some
\sigma
.

We show that, for
L,L^{\prime}
from Table
I
, the flattenings
\mathit{fl}
and
\mathit{fl}_{\sigma_{c}}
defined in the previous section provide uniform separators iff such separators exist. The following example is easily verified using the results to be established below.

Example 25
.

Let
\varphi=\Diamond_{R}^{\geq k}A
. The formula
\mathit{fl}(\varphi)=\Diamond_{R}A
is a uniform
\mathsf{ML\!^{i,n,u}}(\sigma)
-separator for
\varphi
if
\sigma
contains infinitely-many unary and binary predicates (including
R
and
A
) and no constants. If
\sigma_{\ell}=\sigma\cup\sigma_{c}^{\ell}
, where
\sigma_{c}^{\ell}
contains
\ell
-many constants, then
\varphi_{\ell}=\mathit{fl}_{\sigma_{c}^{\ell}}(\varphi)
is a uniform
\mathsf{ML\!^{i,n,u}}(\sigma_{\ell})
-separator for
\varphi
. Note that
\varphi_{\ell^{\prime}}\models\varphi_{\ell}
, for
\sigma_{c}^{\ell^{\prime}}\supseteq\sigma_{c}^{\ell}
, but the converse entailment does not hold. There is no uniform
\mathsf{ML\!^{i,n,u}}(\sigma_{\omega})
-separator for
\varphi
if
\sigma_{\omega}
contains infinitely-many constants.

\dashv

Following what is customary for uniform interpolation and most robust
[
49
,
50
,
51
]
,
we assume that
\sigma
contains countably-infinite sets of unary and binary predicates. If
L
admits nominals, we consider
\sigma
with either a finite or an infinite set of constants, denoted
\sigma_{c}
. Unless otherwise indicated, we assume
\sigma
to be fixed.
We first consider nominal-free logics.

Theorem 26
.

Let
L/L^{\prime}
be either
\mathsf{C^{2}}/\mathsf{FO^{2}_{\neq}}
or any pair of nominal-free modal logics from Table
I
. Then, for any signature
\sigma
with
\sigma_{c}=\emptyset
and any
L(\sigma)
-formula
\varphi
, the following conditions are equivalent
:

(a)

\varphi
has a uniform
L^{\prime}(\sigma)
-separator
;

(b)

\mathit{fl}(\varphi)
is a uniform
L^{\prime}(\sigma)
-separator for
\varphi
;

(c)

\varphi\models\mathit{fl}(\varphi)
;

(d)

\varphi
is preserved under
\omega
-expansions of
\sigma
-structures
\mathfrak{A}
in the sense that
\mathfrak{A}\models\varphi(a)
implies
\mathfrak{A}^{\omega}\models\varphi(a,0)
, for any pointed
\sigma
-structure
\mathfrak{A},a
.

Proof.

We prove
(a)\Rightarrow(d)
for
\mathsf{GML\!^{u}}/\mathsf{ML\!^{u}}
. The implications
(d)\Rightarrow(c)\Rightarrow(b)\Rightarrow(a)
can be proved using Lemmas
17
and
18
; the remaining pairs
L/L^{\prime}
are considered in the appendix.
For a proof by contradiction, suppose
\varphi^{\prime}
is a uniform
\mathsf{ML\!^{u}}(\sigma)
-separator for
\varphi
but there is a pointed
\sigma
-structure
\mathfrak{A},a
such that
\mathfrak{A}\models\varphi(a)
and
\mathfrak{A}^{\omega}\not\models\varphi(a,0)
. By Lemma
18
,
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}\not\models\varphi(a,0)
for all
\kappa>k^{\bullet}
. By Lemma
17
,
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}\models\varphi^{\prime}(a,0)
for all
\kappa>k^{\bullet}
.

For any signature
\varrho
and any
k\geq 0
, let
\mathsf{ML\!^{u}}_{k}(\varrho)
be the set of all
\mathsf{ML\!^{u}}(\varrho)
-formulas of modal depth at most
k
. If
\varrho
is finite, then
\mathsf{ML\!^{u}}_{k}(\varrho)
is finite modulo logical equivalence. Take propositional variables
A_{0},\ldots,A_{k^{\bullet}}
in
\sigma\setminus(\mathit{sig}(\varphi)\cup\mathit{sig}(\varphi^{\prime}))
and let
\varrho=\{A_{0},\ldots,A_{k^{\bullet}}\}\cup\mathit{sig}(\varphi)\cup\mathit{sig}(\varphi^{\prime})
. Define
\mathfrak{B}^{k^{\bullet}+1}
as
\mathfrak{A}^{k^{\bullet}+1}
with the exception that, for
0\leq i\leq k^{\bullet}
,

A_{i}^{\mathfrak{B}^{k^{\bullet}+1}}=\{(a,i)\mid a\in{\sf dom}(\mathfrak{A})\}.

Consider the set
\Gamma
of
\mathsf{ML\!^{u}}_{\textit{md}(\varphi)+2}(\varrho)
-formulas
\chi
such that
\mathfrak{B}^{k^{\bullet}+1}\models\chi(a,0)
. Let
\gamma=\bigwedge_{\chi\in\Gamma}\chi
.
We show that
\gamma\models\varphi\leftrightarrow\mathit{fl}(\varphi)
.
Observe that
\Gamma
contains

\blacksquare(A_{i}\rightarrow\neg A_{j})
, for
0\leq i<j\leq k^{\bullet}
,
and, for every
\chi\in\textit{sub}(\mathit{fl}(\varphi))
and
R\in\varrho
, it also contains

\blacksquare\big[\Diamond_{R}\chi\rightarrow\big(\Diamond_{R}(\chi\wedge A_{0})\wedge\dots\wedge\Diamond_{R}(\chi\wedge A_{k^{\bullet}})\big)\big].

It follows that
\gamma\models\blacksquare(\Diamond_{R}\chi\rightarrow\Diamond_{R}^{\geq k^{\bullet}+1}\chi)
, for all
\chi\in\textit{sub}(\mathit{fl}(\varphi))
and
R\in\varrho
,
and one can show by induction that
\gamma\models\varphi\leftrightarrow\mathit{fl}(\varphi)
.

It follows that
\varphi\models\gamma\rightarrow\mathit{fl}(\varphi)
, and so, by the definition of uniform
\mathsf{ML\!^{u}}(\sigma)
-separators, we have
\varphi^{\prime}\models\gamma\rightarrow\mathit{fl}(\varphi)
. In view of
\mathfrak{B}^{k^{\bullet}+1}\models\varphi^{\prime}(a,0)
and
\mathfrak{B}^{k^{\bullet}+1}\models\gamma(a,0)
, we obtain
\mathfrak{B}^{k^{\bullet}+1}\models\mathit{fl}(\varphi)(a,0)
. Therefore,
\mathfrak{A}^{k^{\bullet}+1}\models\mathit{fl}(\varphi)(a,0)
as
\mathit{fl}(\varphi)
does not contain
A_{0},\ldots,A_{k^{\bullet}}
. It follows from Lemma
18
that
\mathfrak{A}^{k^{\bullet}+1}\models\varphi(a,0)
, which is impossible.

\dashv

By Theorem
26
(b), uniform separator existence is polytime reducible to
L
-validity.
We next discuss the case of modal logics with nominals. We first note a straightforward extension of Theorem
26
to signatures
\sigma
with
finite

\sigma_{c}\neq\emptyset
:

Theorem 27
.

Let
L
and
L^{\prime}
be any pair of modal logics with nominals from Table
I
. Let
\sigma
be a signature with finite
\sigma_{c}\neq\emptyset
. Then conditions
(a)
–
(d)
of Theorem
26
with
\mathit{fl}
replaced by
\mathit{fl}_{\sigma_{c}}
are equivalent to each other.

We illustrate Theorem
27
by an example.

Example 28
.

Suppose
\sigma_{c}=\emptyset
and consider the
\mathsf{GML\!^{n}}
-formula
\varphi=\Diamond_{R}A\wedge\Diamond_{S}A\wedge(\Diamond_{R}^{\geq 2}A\leftrightarrow\Diamond_{S}^{\geq 2}A)
. Then
\mathit{fl}(\varphi)\equiv\Diamond_{R}A\wedge\Diamond_{S}A
,
\varphi\models\mathit{fl}(\varphi)
, and so
\mathit{fl}(\varphi)
is a uniform
\mathsf{ML\!^{n}}(\sigma)
-separator for
\varphi
.
However, if
\sigma_{c}=\{c_{1},c_{2}\}
, then
\varphi\models\neg\mathit{fl}_{\sigma_{c}}(\varphi)
as shown below,

\varphi
\neg\mathit{fl}_{\sigma_{c}}(\varphi)
A
A
N_{c_{1}},N_{c_{2}}
S
R

and so there is no uniform
\mathsf{ML\!^{n}}(\sigma)
-separator for
\varphi
.
\dashv

Finally, we consider the case when the set
\sigma_{c}
of constants in
\sigma
is infinite. We write
\varphi\models_{\it fin}\psi
if

\mathfrak{A}\models\varphi(a)
implies
\mathfrak{A}\models\psi(a)
, for all
finite
pointed
\sigma
-structures
\mathfrak{A},a
. Note that
\varphi\models_{\it fin}\psi
iff
\varphi\models\psi
, for all counting-free logics considered in this paper and also for
\mathsf{GML}
,

\mathsf{GML\!^{n}}
,
\mathsf{GML\!^{u}}
, and
\mathsf{GML\!^{n,u}}
because these logics enjoy the
finite model property
(FMP). All other logics we deal with here do not have the FMP
[
52
,
12
]
.

Theorem 29
.

Let
L
and
L^{\prime}
be any pair of modal logics with nominals from Table
I
. Let
\sigma
be a signature with
\sigma_{c}
infinite and
\sigma_{0}\subseteq\sigma_{c}
finite, non-empty, and containing all constants in an
L(\sigma)
-formula
\varphi
. Then the following conditions are equivalent
:

(a)

\varphi
has a uniform
L^{\prime}(\sigma)
-separator
;

(b)

\mathit{fl}_{\sigma_{0}}(\varphi)
is a uniform
L^{\prime}(\sigma)
-separator for
\varphi
;

(c)

\varphi\models\mathit{fl}_{\sigma_{0}}(\varphi)
and
\mathit{fl}_{\sigma_{0}}(\varphi)\models_{\it fin}\varphi
.

Proof.

We sketch the proof of (b)
\Rightarrow
(c) for
\mathsf{GML\!^{i,n,u}}/\mathsf{ML\!^{i,n,u}}
;
(c)\Rightarrow(b)
is straightforward and
(a)\Leftrightarrow(b)
is shown using Theorem
27
. Observe that, for a finite pointed
\sigma_{f}
-structure
\mathfrak{A},a
with finite
\sigma_{f}
, one can construct an
\mathsf{ML\!^{i,n,u}}(\sigma)
-formula
\text{diag}_{\sigma_{f}}(\mathfrak{A},a)
with fresh constants for elements of
{\sf dom}(\mathfrak{A})
such that
(i)
if
\mathfrak{B}\models\text{diag}_{\sigma_{f}}(\mathfrak{A},a)(b)
, then
\mathfrak{A},a\cong_{\sigma_{f}}\mathfrak{B},b
and
(ii)
by interpreting the fresh constants in
\text{diag}_{\sigma_{f}}(\mathfrak{A},a)
as elements of
{\sf dom}(\mathfrak{A})
,

\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}\models\text{diag}_{\sigma_{f}}(\mathfrak{A},a)(a)
for the resulting structure
\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}
.
Let
\mathit{fl}_{\sigma_{0}}(\varphi)
be a uniform
L^{\prime}(\sigma)
-separator for
\varphi
, so

\varphi\models\mathit{fl}_{\sigma_{0}}(\varphi)
. To show

\mathit{fl}_{\sigma_{0}}(\varphi)\models_{\it fin}\varphi
, assume this is not the case and there is a finite pointed
\mathit{sig}(\varphi)\cup\sigma_{0}
-structure
\mathfrak{A},a
with
\mathfrak{A}\models\mathit{fl}_{\sigma_{0}}(\varphi)(a)
but

\mathfrak{A}\not\models\varphi(a)
.
Then
\varphi\models\neg\text{diag}_{\sigma_{f}}(\mathfrak{A},a)
for
\sigma_{f}=\mathit{sig}(\varphi)\cup\sigma_{0}
.
By the definition of uniform separators,
\mathit{fl}_{\sigma_{0}}(\varphi)\models\neg\text{diag}_{\sigma_{f}}(\mathfrak{A},a)
, and so
\mathfrak{A}\not\models\mathit{fl}_{\sigma_{0}}(\varphi)(a)
, which is a contradiction.

\dashv

It follows that, for the pairs
L/L^{\prime}
considered in Theorem
29
, uniform
L^{\prime}(\sigma)
-separator existence for
\sigma
containing infinitely many constants reduces in polytime to finite and general
L
-validity. For logics
L
with the FMP, it follows from Theorem
29
that uniform
L^{\prime}
-separator existence and
L^{\prime}
-definability coincide. For
L
without the FMP, this is not the case.

Example 30
.

Consider
\mathsf{GML\!^{i,n}}/\mathsf{ML\!^{i,n}}
. Let
\varphi
be a satisfiable
\mathsf{GML\!^{i,n}}
-formula that is not finitely satisfiable. We claim that
\top
is a uniform
\mathsf{ML\!^{i,n}}(\sigma)
-separator for
\neg\varphi
. Indeed, if
\neg\varphi\models\chi
, for some
\mathsf{ML\!^{i,n}}
-formula
\chi
, then
\neg\chi\models\varphi
, and so
\neg\chi
is not finitely satisfiable. By the FMP of
\mathsf{ML\!^{i,n}}
, this can only happen if
\neg\chi
is not satisfiable, that is,
\chi\equiv\top
. On the other hand,
\neg\varphi
is not
\mathsf{ML\!^{i,n}}(\sigma)
-definable since
\mathsf{ML\!^{i,n}}
enjoys the FMP and so
\varphi
would be finitely satisfiable iff it is satisfiable.
\dashv

We close with a brief discussion of uniform
\mathsf{C^{2}}/\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}
-separation, which reduces to uniform
\mathsf{FO^{2}_{\neq}}/\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}
-separation since we have already analysed uniform
\mathsf{C^{2}}/\mathsf{FO^{2}_{\neq}}
-separation. Surprisingly, we can again use the same function
m(\varphi)
that characterises
\mathsf{FO^{2}_{\neq}}/\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}
-definability. In this case, we show in the appendix that, for any
\mathsf{FO^{2}_{\neq}}(\sigma)
-formula
\varphi
, the following conditions are equivalent:

(a)

\varphi
has a uniform suc
\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}(\sigma)
-separator;

(b)

m(\varphi)
is a uniform suc
\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}(\sigma)
-separator for
\varphi
;

(c)

\varphi\models m(\varphi)
.

It follows that uniform
\mathsf{FO^{2}_{\neq}}/\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}(\sigma)
-separation can be reduced in polytime to
\mathsf{FO^{2}_{\neq}}
-validity. In contrast, for
\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}/\mathsf{ML\!^{i}}
and
\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}/\mathsf{ML}
, the definability results do not directly transfer to uniform separation; their analysis is left for future work.

IX
Future Work

We have presented a comprehensive analysis of the complexity of separation and definability in fragments of
\mathsf{C^{2}}
. While a rather elementary (but elegant) approach worked in many cases for definability and uniform separation, more labour was needed to understand separation. Nevertheless, the decidability of
\mathsf{C^{2}}/\mathsf{FO^{2}}
-definability and uniform separation remain open and will require a different technique compared to the decidability proof for
\mathsf{C^{2}}/\mathsf{FO^{2}_{\neq}}
.

As far as separation is concerned, a main open problem is to find methods to construct separators and bound their size. Based on the mosaic technique introduced in this article, first results have been obtained
[
53
]
. Using a mosaic elimination algorithm, it is shown that for
\mathsf{GML}/\mathsf{ML}
- and
\mathsf{GML\!^{u}}/\mathsf{ML\!^{u}}
-separation, one can always construct separators of elementary size if separators exist at all.

It would also be of interest to investigate the problems considered here over finite models. If
L
has the FMP, then there is no difference, so only
\mathsf{C^{2}}
and extensions of
\mathsf{GML\!^{i,u}}
are affected. We conjecture that it is straightforward to adapt our undecidability proofs by working with appropriate ‘finitary’
n
-bisimulations (as compactness cannot be used, Theorem
4
does not hold and has to be replaced by a finitary approximation). The definability proofs are easily adapted, and so are the proofs for uniform separation. Observe that now definability coincides with uniform separation for all pairs of logics
L/L^{\prime}
with nominals in Table
I
if signatures containing infinitely-many constants are considered. Significant modifications are needed, however, to deal with
\mathsf{GML\!^{i,u}}/\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}
-separation, where the current proof relies on realising star-types infinitely often.

Proofs for Section
III

See
5

Proof.

If
L\in\{\mathsf{FO^{2}},\mathsf{C^{2}}\}
and
\varphi(x),\psi(x)\in L
, we define
L
-formulas
\varphi^{\prime}(x)
and
\psi^{\prime}(x)
by taking

\displaystyle\varphi^{\prime}(x)~=~\varphi(x)\land\hskip-5.69054pt\bigwedge_{A\in\mathit{sig}(\psi)\setminus\mathit{sig}(\varphi)}\hskip-17.07164pt\big(A(x)\to A(x)\big)\land{}

\displaystyle\hskip 62.59596pt\bigwedge_{R\in\mathit{sig}(\psi)\setminus\mathit{sig}(\varphi)}\hskip-17.07164pt\big(R(x,x)\to R(x,x)\big)\land\hskip-5.69054pt\bigwedge_{c\in\mathit{sig}(\psi)\setminus\mathit{sig}(\varphi)}\hskip-17.07164pt(c=c),

\displaystyle\psi^{\prime}(x)~=~\psi(x)\land\hskip-5.69054pt\bigwedge_{A\in\mathit{sig}(\varphi)\setminus\mathit{sig}(\psi)}\hskip-17.07164pt\big(A(x)\to A(x)\big)\land{}

\displaystyle\hskip 62.59596pt\bigwedge_{R\in\mathit{sig}(\varphi)\setminus\mathit{sig}(\psi)}\hskip-17.07164pt\big(R(x,x)\to R(x,x)\big)\land\hskip-5.69054pt\bigwedge_{c\in\mathit{sig}(\varphi)\setminus\mathit{sig}(\psi)}\hskip-17.07164pt(c=c).

Then
\varphi(x)
and
\psi(x)
are
L/L^{\prime}
-separable iff
\varphi^{\prime}(x)
and
\psi^{\prime}(x)
are Craig
L/L^{\prime}
-separable, and so unary Craig separable.
If
L
is a modal logic, we take
\Diamond_{R}\top\to\Diamond_{R}\top
and
N_{c}\to N_{c}
in the last two conjuncts of
\varphi^{\prime}(x)
and
\psi^{\prime}(x)
.

For the second claim, let
\mathfrak{A}
be a
\sigma
-structure. For a non-empty set
D\subseteq{\sf dom}(\mathfrak{A})
, denote by
\mathfrak{A}_{|D}
the
restriction
of
\mathfrak{A}
to
D
defined by taking
{\sf dom}(\mathfrak{A}_{|D})=D
and by relativising all predicates in
\sigma
to
D
, that is, by setting
(a_{1},\dots,a_{n})\in R^{\mathfrak{A}_{|D}}
iff
a_{1},\dots,a_{n}\in D
and
(a_{1},\dots,a_{n})\in R^{\mathfrak{A}}
, for all
a_{1},\dots,a_{n}\in{\sf dom}(\mathfrak{A})
and all
R\in\sigma
. If
\sigma
contains constants
c
, we demand that
c^{\mathfrak{A}}\in D
and set
c^{\mathfrak{A}_{|D}}=c^{\mathfrak{A}}
.
We remind the reader that the
relativisation

\varphi_{|A}(x)
of a formula
\varphi(x)
to a unary predicate
A\notin\mathit{sig}(\varphi)
has the following property
[
42
]
: for all structures
\mathfrak{A}
and all
a\in{\sf dom}(\mathfrak{A})
,

–

\mathfrak{A}\models\varphi_{|A}(a)
implies
A^{\mathfrak{A}}\neq\emptyset
;

–

\mathfrak{A}\models\varphi_{|A}(a)
iff
\mathfrak{A}_{|A^{\mathfrak{A}}}\models\varphi(a)
.

For a
\mathsf{C^{2}}
-formula
\varphi(x)
, its
\mathsf{C^{2}}
-relativisation
\varphi(x)_{|A}
is constructed in polynomial time.

We are now in a position to give the reduction
for
\mathsf{C^{2}}/\mathsf{FO^{2}}
. Assume
\varphi(x)
and
\psi(x)
in
\mathsf{C^{2}}
are given. Let
A_{1},\ldots,A_{n}
be the unary predicate symbols that either occur in
\varphi
and not in
\psi
or vice versa. Let
\varrho=(\mathit{sig}(\varphi)\cup\mathit{sig}(\psi))\setminus\{A_{1},\dots,A_{n}\}
. Let

U
be a fresh unary predicate symbol and
R
a fresh binary predicate symbol.
Let
\varphi_{|U}
and
\psi_{|U}
be the relativisations of
\varphi
and
\psi
to
U
.
Consider the formulas
\varphi^{\prime}
and
\psi^{\prime}
obtained by
replacing in
\varphi_{|U}
and
\psi_{|U}
every occurrence of
A_{i}
by a formula
\varphi_{A_{i}}
stating that there is an
R
-chain of length
i
with the final node having exactly two
R
-successors. In
\mathsf{GML}
terms,

\varphi_{A_{i}}=\underbrace{\Diamond_{R}\dots\Diamond_{R}}_{i}\Diamond_{R}^{=2}\top.

Let
\tau=\mathit{sig}(\varphi^{\prime})\cup\mathit{sig}(\psi^{\prime})
. By Lemma
4
, it suffices to show that the following conditions are equivalent:

1.

there exist
\mathfrak{A},a
and
\mathfrak{B},b
such that
\mathfrak{A}\models\varphi(a)
,
\mathfrak{B}\models\psi(b)
, and
\mathfrak{A},a\sim_{\mathsf{FO^{2}}(\varrho)}\mathfrak{B},b
;

2.

there exist
\mathfrak{A}^{\prime},a^{\prime}
and
\mathfrak{B}^{\prime},b^{\prime}
such that
\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}\models\varphi^{\prime}(a^{\prime})
,
\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}\models\psi^{\prime}(b^{\prime})
, and
\mathfrak{A}^{\prime},a^{\prime}\sim_{\mathsf{FO^{2}}(\tau)}\mathfrak{B}^{\prime},b^{\prime}
.

For the implication
(1)\Rightarrow(2)
, suppose

\mathfrak{A}\models\varphi(a)
,
\mathfrak{B}\models\psi(b)
and
\mathfrak{A},a\sim_{\mathsf{FO^{2}}(\varrho)}\mathfrak{A},b
.
Define
\mathfrak{A}^{\prime},a^{\prime}
and
\mathfrak{B}^{\prime},b^{\prime}
as follows: the domain of

\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}
contains
{\sf dom}(\mathfrak{A})
and, for every
d\in{\sf dom}(\mathfrak{A})
, mutually disjoint sets
A_{d}
we define later.
We set
a^{\prime}=a
. Similarly, the domain of

\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}
contains
{\sf dom}(\mathfrak{B})
and, for every
d\in{\sf dom}(\mathfrak{B})
, mutually disjoint sets
B_{d}
to be defined later. Set
b^{\prime}=b
.
The symbols in
\varrho
are interpreted in
\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}
and
\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}
exactly as in
\mathfrak{A}
and
\mathfrak{B}
, respectively. Set
U^{\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}}={\sf dom}(\mathfrak{A})
and
U^{\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}}={\sf dom}(\mathfrak{B})
.
It remains to interpret
R
. We interpret
R
in
\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}
in such a way that, for
d\in{\sf dom}(\mathfrak{A})
, we have
\mathfrak{A}\models A_{i}(d)
iff
\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}\models\varphi_{A_{i}}(d)
, for
i=1,\dots,n
. Hence, we use the additional points in
A_{d}
as the domain of an
\{R\}
-structure
\mathfrak{A}_{d}
consisting of
n
-many
R
-chains all starting at
d
, with the
i
th
R
-chain having length
i
and such that its final node has
two

R
-successors if
d\in A_{i}^{\mathfrak{A}}
and
three

R
-successors if
d\not\in A_{i}^{\mathfrak{A}}
. For
d\in{\sf dom}(\mathfrak{A})
, the
\mathfrak{A}_{d}
are hooked to
\mathfrak{A}
at

to obtain
\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}
. Observe that we have
\mathfrak{A}_{d}\models\varphi_{A_{i}}(d)
iff
\mathfrak{A}\models A_{i}(d)
, for
i=1,\dots,n
, and that
\mathfrak{A}_{d},d\sim_{\mathsf{FO^{2}}(\{R\})}\mathfrak{A}_{d^{\prime}},d^{\prime}
, for any
d,d^{\prime}\in{\sf dom}(\mathfrak{A})
, since
\mathsf{FO^{2}}
cannot distinguish between nodes having two or three
R
-successors.
The interpretation of
R
in
\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}
is analogous.

It follows that
\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}\models\varphi^{\prime}(a^{\prime})
and
\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}\models\psi^{\prime}(b^{\prime})
. Moreover, any
\boldsymbol{\beta}
witnessing
\mathfrak{A},a\sim_{\mathsf{FO^{2}}(\varrho)}\mathfrak{B},b
can be extended to a
\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\prime}
witnessing
\mathfrak{A}^{\prime},a^{\prime}\sim_{\mathsf{FO^{2}}(\tau)}\mathfrak{B}^{\prime},b^{\prime}

in a straightforward manner.

For
(2)\Rightarrow(1)
, assume that

\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}\models\varphi^{\prime}(a^{\prime})
,
\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}\models\psi^{\prime}(b^{\prime})
, and
\mathfrak{A}^{\prime},a^{\prime}\sim_{\mathsf{FO^{2}}(\tau)}\mathfrak{B}^{\prime},b^{\prime}
.
Let
\mathfrak{A}=\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}_{|U^{\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}}}
be expanded by setting

A_{i}^{\mathfrak{A}}=\{d\in U^{\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}}\mid\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}\models\varphi_{A_{i}}(d)\},

for
i=1,\dots,n
, and let
\mathfrak{B}=\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}_{|U^{\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}}}
be expanded by setting

A_{i}^{\mathfrak{B}}=\{d\in U^{\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}}\mid\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}\models\varphi_{A_{i}}(d)\},

for
i=1,\dots,n
. Let
a=a^{\prime}
and
b=b^{\prime}
. By construction, we have
\mathfrak{A}\models\varphi(a)
and
\mathfrak{B}\models\psi(b)
. Moreover, let
\boldsymbol{\beta}
be a bisimulation witnessing
\mathfrak{A}^{\prime},a^{\prime}\sim_{\mathsf{FO^{2}}(\tau)}\mathfrak{B}^{\prime},b^{\prime}
. Then the restriction of
\boldsymbol{\beta}
to
U^{\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}}\times U^{\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}}
witnesses
\mathfrak{A},a\sim_{\mathsf{FO^{2}}(\varrho)}\mathfrak{B},b
.

The polynomial reduction given above works for all pairs
\mathsf{C^{2}}/L^{\prime}
with
L^{\prime}\in\{\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}},\mathsf{ML\!^{i}},\mathsf{ML\!^{u}},\mathsf{ML}\}
since the formulas
\varphi_{A_{i}}
are in
\mathsf{C^{2}}
.

Next consider
\mathsf{GML\!^{i,n,u}}/\mathsf{ML\!^{i,n,u}}
. As before, we can construct the relativisation
\varphi_{|A}
of a
\mathsf{GML\!^{i,n,u}}
-formula
\varphi
in
\mathsf{GML\!^{i,n,u}}
to a propositional variable
A
. To ensure that all nominals are interpreted in the relativised structure, we add to
\varphi_{|A}
the conjunct
\Diamondblack(N_{c}\wedge A)
for
c\in\mathit{sig}(\varphi)\cup\mathit{sig}(\psi)
. With this amendments, the proof above works for
\mathsf{GML\!^{i,n,u}}/L^{\prime}
and all
L^{\prime}\in\{\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}},\mathsf{ML\!^{i,n}},\mathsf{ML\!^{n,u}},\mathsf{ML\!^{u}},\mathsf{ML\!^{i}},\mathsf{ML\!^{n}},\mathsf{ML}\}
.

The proof for the relevant pairs
\mathsf{GML\!^{i,u}}/L^{\prime}
,
\mathsf{GML\!^{n,u}}/L^{\prime}
,
\mathsf{GML\!^{u}}/L^{\prime}
,
\mathsf{GML\!^{i}}/L^{\prime}
, and
\mathsf{GML}/L^{\prime}
is a straightforward modification of the proof above.

For the relevant pairs of the form
\mathsf{GML\!^{i,n}}/L^{\prime}
and
\mathsf{GML\!^{n}}/L^{\prime}
, some care is required since one cannot now ensure that nominals are interpreted in the relativised structure by adding
\Diamondblack(N_{c}\wedge U)
as a conjunct to
\varphi_{|U}
.
Instead, when constructing
\mathfrak{A}
and
\mathfrak{B}
from
\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}_{|U^{\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}}}
and
\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}_{|U^{\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}}}
in the proof of
(2)\Rightarrow(1)
, we add new elements to the domains of
\mathfrak{A}
and
\mathfrak{B}
to interpret the nominals not yet interpreted in
\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}_{|U^{\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}}}
and
\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}_{|U^{\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}}}
.

\dashv

The following criterion of relative definability is proved similarly to Lemma
4
:

Lemma 31
.

For any relevant pair
L/L^{\prime}
, any
L(\sigma)
-formulas
\varphi(x)
,
\psi(x)
and any
\varrho\subseteq\sigma
, the following conditions are equivalent
:

–

there is no explicit
L^{\prime}(\varrho)
-definition of
\psi
modulo
\varphi
;

–

there are pointed
\sigma
-structures
\mathfrak{A},a
and
\mathfrak{B},b
such that

\mathfrak{A}\models(\varphi\land\psi)(a),\quad\mathfrak{B}\models(\varphi\land\neg\psi)(b),\quad\mathfrak{A},a\sim_{L^{\prime}(\varrho)}\mathfrak{B},b.

See
6

Proof.

We only consider modal logics
L
and
L^{\prime}
; the first-order case is similar. Let
\varrho\subseteq\sigma
. We show that an
L
-formula
\psi
has an explicit
L^{\prime}(\varrho)
-definition modulo
\varphi
in
L
iff the formulas
\varphi\land\psi
and
\varphi^{\varrho}\land\neg\psi^{\varrho}
have a Craig
L/L^{\prime}
-separator, where
\varphi^{\varrho}
,
\psi^{\varrho}
are obtained by replacing each variable
A\notin\varrho
with a fresh variable
A^{\varrho}
(cf.
[
46
]
). Indeed, suppose first that
\chi
is an
L^{\prime}(\varrho)
-formula with
\varphi\models\psi\leftrightarrow\chi
. Let
\chi^{\prime}
be the result of replacing every variable in
\chi
that does not occur in
\mathit{sig}(\varphi\land\psi)
by
\top
, so
\mathit{sig}(\chi^{\prime})\subseteq\mathit{sig}(\varphi\land\psi)\cup\mathit{sig}(\varphi^{\varrho}\land\psi^{\varrho})
. We clearly still have
\varphi\models\psi\leftrightarrow\chi^{\prime}
, and so
\varphi\land\psi\models\chi^{\prime}
and
\chi^{\prime}\models\varphi\to\psi
. The latter implies
\chi^{\prime}\models\varphi^{\varrho}\to\psi^{\varrho}
and
\chi^{\prime}\models\neg(\varphi^{\varrho}\land\neg\psi^{\varrho})
. Therefore,
\chi^{\prime}
is a Craig
L/L^{\prime}
-separator for
\varphi\land\psi
and
\varphi^{\varrho}\land\neg\psi^{\varrho}
.
Conversely, it is readily seen that any Craig
L^{\prime}
-separator of
\varphi\land\psi
and
\varphi^{\varrho}\wedge\neg\psi^{\varrho}
is an explicit
L^{\prime}(\varrho)
-definition of
\psi
modulo
\varphi
.

For the other reduction, we observe first that the decision problem for
L
is polynomial time reducible to relative
L/L^{\prime}
-definability: for
\psi=A\notin\mathit{sig}(\varphi)
and
\varrho=\emptyset
, we have
\models\neg\varphi
iff there is an explicit
L^{\prime}(\varrho)
-definition of
\psi
modulo
\varphi
.
We show now that
L
-formulas
\varphi
and
\neg\psi
have a Craig
L/L^{\prime}
-separator iff
\models\varphi\to\psi
and there is an
L^{\prime}(\mathit{sig}(\varphi)\cap\mathit{sig}(\psi))
-definition of
\psi
modulo
\psi\to\varphi
.

Indeed, if
\chi
is a Craig
L/L^{\prime}
-separator for
\varphi
and
\neg\psi
, then we have
\mathit{sig}(\chi)\subseteq\mathit{sig}(\varphi)\cap\mathit{sig}(\psi)
,
\varphi\models\chi
and
\chi\models\psi
. It follows that
\models\varphi\to\psi
and
\psi\to\varphi\models\psi\leftrightarrow\chi
, which means that
\chi
is an
L^{\prime}(\mathit{sig}(\varphi)\cap\mathit{sig}(\psi))
-definition of
\psi
modulo
\psi\to\varphi
. Conversely, suppose
\models\varphi\to\psi
but
\varphi
and
\neg\psi
do not have a Craig
L/L^{\prime}
-separator. By Lemma
4
, there are structures
\mathfrak{A},a
and
\mathfrak{B},b
such that
\mathfrak{A}\models\varphi(a)
,
\mathfrak{B}\models\neg\psi(b)
and
\mathfrak{A},a\sim_{L^{\prime}(\varrho)}\mathfrak{B},b
. But then
\mathfrak{A}\models((\psi\to\varphi)\land\psi)(a)
and
\mathfrak{B}\models((\psi\to\varphi)\land\neg\psi)(b)
, and so, by Lemma
31
, there is no explicit
L^{\prime}(\varrho)
-definition of
\psi
modulo
\psi\to\varphi
.

\dashv

Proofs for Section
IV

See
7

-A

\mathsf{GML\!^{i,n,u}}/\mathsf{ML\!^{i,n,u}}
-separation

We reduce the non-halting problem for 2RMs to the non-existence of a Craig
\mathsf{GML\!^{i,n,u}}/\mathsf{ML\!^{i,n,u}}
-separator. Recall that a 2RM is a pair

M=(Q,P)
, where
Q=q_{0},\dots,q_{m}
is a set of
states

and
P=I_{0},\dots,I_{m-1}
a sequence of
instructions
.
By definition,
q_{0}
is the
initial state
, and
q_{m}
the

halting state
. For all
i<m
,

–

either
I_{i}=+(\ell,q_{j})
is an
incrementation instruction
with a register
\ell\in\{0,1\}
and the subsequent state
q_{j}
;

–

or
I_{i}=-(\ell,q_{j},q_{k})
is a
decrementation instruction
with a register
\ell\in\{0,1\}
, the subsequent state
q_{j}
if register
\ell
contains 0, and
q_{k}
otherwise.

A
configuration
of
M
is a triple
(q,n_{0},n_{1})
, where
q
is the
current state and
n_{0},n_{1}\in\omega
the register contents. We
write
(q_{i},n_{0},n_{1})\Rightarrow_{M}(q_{j},n_{0}^{\prime},n_{1}^{\prime})
if one of the
following holds:

–

I_{i}=+(\ell,q_{j})
,
n_{\ell}^{\prime}=n_{\ell}+1
, and
n_{1-\ell}^{\prime}=n_{1-\ell}
;

–

I_{i}=-(\ell,q_{j},q_{k})
,
n_{\ell}^{\prime}=n_{\ell}=0
, and
n_{1-\ell}^{\prime}=n_{1-\ell}
;

–

I_{i}=-(\ell,q_{k},q_{j})
,
n_{\ell}>0
,
n^{\prime}_{\ell}=n_{\ell}-1
, and
n_{1-\ell}^{\prime}=n_{1-\ell}
.

The
computation
of
M
on input
(n_{0},n_{1})\in\omega^{2}
is
the unique longest configuration sequence

(p^{0},k_{0}^{0},k_{1}^{0})\Rightarrow_{M}(p^{1},k_{0}^{1},k_{1}^{1})\Rightarrow_{M}\cdots

such that
p^{0}=q_{0}
,
k_{0}^{0}=n_{0}
, and
k_{1}^{0}=n_{1}
.
The
halting problem
for 2RMs is to decide, given a 2RM
M
, whether
its computation on input
(0,0)
is finite (which implies that its
last state is
q_{m}
).

We assume without loss of generality (by adding a ‘dummy’ initial state if necessary) that the initial state
q_{0}
only occurs initially and that
I_{0}
is a decrementation instruction.

Since we are reducing the non-halting problem for 2RMs to the non-existence of a Craig
\mathsf{GML\!^{i,n,u}}/\mathsf{ML\!^{i,n,u}}
-separator, any machine
M
will be associated with two
\mathsf{GML\!^{i,n,u}}
-formulas
\varphi_{M}
and
\psi_{M}
. Models of
\varphi_{M}
represent the progression of the states of
M
over time, while models of
\psi_{M}
represent the content of the registers over time. The two are bisimilar (and hence there is no separator) if the state progression and the register contents match, and hence the models of
\varphi_{M}
and
\psi_{M}
together represent the execution of
M
.

The shared signature,
\varrho
, of
\varphi_{M}
and
\psi_{M}
consists of propositional variables
q_{i}\in Q
,
e_{0}
and
e_{1}
, along with constant
c
and binary relation
R
. Furthermore,
\psi_{M}
uses non-shared propositional variables
r_{0}
and
u
. For ease of notation, we define
r_{1}\equiv\neg r_{0}
.

The intended meaning of
q_{i}
is ‘the machine is currently in state
q_{i}
’​, while
e_{0}
and
e_{1}
are used to represent ‘register 0 is empty’ and ‘register 1 is empty’​, respectively. The relation
R
is used to represent the passage of time (i.e., if
(x,y)\in R
, then
y
is one time step later than
x
). The constant
c
identifies the starting point of the computation. The variable
r_{0}
is used to indicate that a particular element of the domain counts as a part of register 0, while
r_{1}
indicates elements that count as part of register 1. Finally,
u
stands for ‘unique’​, and will be true for exactly one element of each register at each point in time.

The two formulas
\varphi_{M}
and
\psi_{M}
are both conjunctions, with the conjuncts best presented in a few groups. For
\varphi_{M}
, the first group of conjuncts is as follows:

1.

N_{c}\wedge q_{0}
,

2.

\blacksquare\bigvee_{1\leq i\leq m}q_{i}
,

3.

\blacksquare\bigwedge_{1\leq i\leq m}(q_{i}\rightarrow\bigwedge_{i<j\leq m}\neg q_{j})
.

Subformulas (
1
)–(
3
) express that every element of the domain satisfies exactly one of
q_{0},\dots,q_{m}
, and the constant
c
is the initial point of evaluation and satisfies
q_{0}
.

The next group of conjuncts of
\varphi_{M}
is as follows:

4.

\blacksquare(\Diamond\!^{=1}_{R}\top)
,

5.

\square_{R^{-}}\bot
,

6.

\blacksquare(\neg N_{c}\rightarrow\Diamond\!^{=1}_{R^{-1}}\top)
.

These conjuncts describe the relation
R
: every element has exactly one
R
-successor, the initial element has no
R
-predecessors, and every other element has exactly one
R
-predecessor.

It follows from these conditions that if
\mathfrak{A}\models\varphi_{M}(x_{0})
, then
\mathfrak{A}
contains an infinite
R
-chain starting at
x_{0}
and each element of the chain satisfies exactly one
q_{i}
. We will interpret these elements as representing a run of
M
.

The next group of conjuncts depends on the instructions of the machine
M
. If
I_{i}=+(\ell,q_{j})
, we add the conjunct

7.

\blacksquare(q_{i}\rightarrow\square_{R}q_{j})
,

and if
I_{i}=-(\ell,q_{j},q_{k})
we add the conjuncts

8.

\blacksquare((q_{i}\wedge e_{\ell})\rightarrow\square_{R}q_{j})
,

9.

\blacksquare((q_{i}\wedge\neg e_{\ell})\rightarrow\square_{R}q_{k})
.

These conjuncts express that the instructions of
M
are followed, where
e_{\ell}
(with
\ell\in\{0,1\}
) indicates that register
\ell
is empty. Note that we do not, at this stage, keep track of the value in each register. But
if

e_{\ell}
holds iff register
\ell
is empty, then these conjuncts ensure that every pointed model
\mathfrak{A},x_{0}
of
\varphi_{M}
encodes the run of
M
.

Finally, we add one more, very simple, conjunct

10.

\blacksquare\neg q_{\ell}
,

which expresses that the halting state
q_{\ell}
is not reached. So, if a model of
\varphi_{M}
encodes a run of
M
, it must be a non-halting run.

The formula
\psi_{M}
can similarly be divided into groups of conjuncts.
The first group of conjuncts of
\psi_{M}
is the same as the the first group of
\varphi_{M}
:

1.

N_{c}\wedge q_{0}
,

2.

\blacksquare\bigvee_{1\leq i\leq m}q_{i}
,

3.

\blacksquare\bigwedge_{1\leq i\leq m}(q_{i}\rightarrow\bigwedge_{i<j\leq m}\neg q_{j})
.

So, as with
\varphi_{M}
, in every model of
\psi_{M}
, all elements satisfy exactly one of
q_{0},\dots,q_{m}
. The remaining conjuncts are quite different, however:

4.

\Diamond\!_{R}^{=2}\top
,

5.

\Diamond\!_{R}(u\wedge r_{0})\wedge\Diamond\!_{R}(u\wedge r_{1})
,

6.

e_{0}\wedge e_{1}
,

7.

\blacksquare(r_{\ell}\rightarrow(\square_{R}r_{\ell}\wedge\square_{R^{-1}}(e_{\ell}\vee N_{c})))
, for
\ell\in\{0,1\}
,

8.

\blacksquare((u\wedge\neg N_{c})\rightarrow(\Diamond\!_{R}^{=1}u\wedge\Diamond\!_{R^{-1}}^{=1}u))
.

Note that conjuncts (
4
)–(
6
) do not start with
\blacksquare
, so they apply only to the initial point of evaluation which, due to conjunct (
1
), is the constant
c
. The conjuncts then state that this element has exactly two
R
-successors, with one satisfying
u\wedge r_{0}
and the other satisfying
u\wedge r_{1}
. Since we use
r_{0}
and
r_{1}
to denote the two registers, this means that a model of
\psi_{M}
splits into two branches representing the two registers. Furthermore, conjunct (
6
) says that
e_{0}
and
e_{1}
both hold at
c
, i.e., both registers are initially empty.

Conjuncts (
7
) and (
8
) handle propagation: (
7
) says that
r_{\ell}
propagates forward and backward along
R
(except for
c
), while (
8
) says that
u
propagates to exactly one
R
-successor and
R
-predecessor (except for
c
). So the branches representing the registers do not interact, and there is a unique
R
-path in each register of elements that satisfy
u
.

The next group of conjuncts depends on the instructions of
M
. For
I_{i}=+(\ell,q_{j})
, we have conjuncts

9.

\blacksquare((q_{i}\wedge r_{\ell})\rightarrow(\Diamond\!_{R}^{=2}\top\wedge\square_{R}\Diamond\!_{R^{-1}}^{=1}\top))
,

10.

\blacksquare((q_{i}\wedge\neg r_{\ell})\rightarrow(\Diamond\!_{R}^{=1}\top\wedge\square_{R}\Diamond\!_{R^{-1}}^{=1}\top))
.

For
i\not=0
and
I_{i}=-(\ell,q_{j},q_{k})
, we have

11.

\blacksquare((q_{i}\wedge r_{\ell}\wedge e_{\ell})\rightarrow(\Diamond\!_{R}^{=1}\top\wedge\square_{R}\Diamond\!_{R^{-1}}^{=1}.\top))
,

12.

\blacksquare((q_{i}\wedge r_{\ell}\wedge\neg e_{\ell})\rightarrow(\Diamond\!_{R}^{=1}\top\wedge\square_{R}\Diamond\!_{R^{-1}}^{=2}.\top))
,

13.

\blacksquare((q_{i}\wedge\neg r_{\ell})\rightarrow(\Diamond\!_{R}^{=1}\top\wedge\square_{R}\Diamond\!_{R^{-1}}^{=1}.\top))
.

Conjunct (
9
) says that if
q_{i}
is an incrementation instruction, then an element satisfying
q_{i}\wedge r_{\ell}
will have two
R
-successors, each of which having one
R
-predecessor. As we use
R
to represent time in the computation, this means that the number of
r_{\ell}
-elements will double in a state with an incrementation instruction. With respect to conjuncts (
11
) and (
12
), recall that we use
e_{\ell}
as a marker for register
\ell
being empty. Thus, these conjuncts say that if a decrementation instruction is given with an empty register (conjunct (
11
)), then for every
r_{\ell}
-element, there is one
R
-successor that has one
R
-predecessor, so the number of
r_{\ell}
-elements remains the same, and if a decrementation instruction is given with a non-empty register (conjunct (
12
)), then for every
r_{\ell}
-element, there is a single
R
-successor that has two
R
-predecessors, so the number of
r_{\ell}
-elements is halved.

Since we double for incrementation and halve for decrementation, a register value of
n
in register
\ell
will be represented by
2^{n}
elements satisfying
r_{\ell}
.

Conjuncts (
10
) and (
13
) state that elements in the other register (i.e., those satisfying
\neg r_{\ell}
) have exactly one successor that has exactly one predecessor, so the number of such elements remains the same.

Note that we treat
q_{0}
separately. This is because we first need to create the two registers before we can increase or decrease the number of elements in each register. This is done by conjuncts (
4
) and (
5
). After one step of the computation, our two registers are initialised with one element each, which we interpret as the value
0
for that register. This is why we assume that
q_{0}
is decrementing (so the registers are empty after one step of the computation) and only occurs initially (so we do not perform the initialisation again at later times).

Finally, we add one more conjunct

14.

\blacksquare((e_{\ell}\sqcap r_{\ell})\rightarrow u)
,

which states that the combination of
e_{\ell}
and
r_{\ell}
is possible only when
u
also holds.

We show that an
\mathsf{ML\!^{i,n,u}}
-separator for
\varphi_{M}
and
\psi_{M}
exists iff
M
is non-halting, from which it follows that separation is undecidable. More precisely, the reduction shows that non-separation is co-RE hard, and therefore the separation problem is RE hard.

Before proving our reduction, let us first introduce the
intended pointed models
for
\varphi_{M}
and
\psi_{M}
, where
M
is non-halting. A pointed model
\mathfrak{A},x_{0}
of
\varphi_{M}
will be used to represent a clock, and the state the machine is in at each time step. Recall that
\varphi_{M}
guarantees that
\mathfrak{A}
follows the instructions of
M
as long as
e_{\ell}
holds when register
\ell
is empty. In our intended models, we will make sure that (i)
e_{\ell}
only holds if register
\ell
is empty and (ii) if register
\ell
is empty and the current instruction is to decrement register
\ell
, then
e_{\ell}
holds (to indicate that decrementing is impossible). If register
\ell
is empty and the current instruction is anything other than to decrement register
\ell
, we do not care whether
e_{\ell}
holds.

A pointed model
\mathfrak{B},y_{0}
of
\psi_{M}
is used to represent the content of the registers. Every point
t
of the computation is represented by some element
x_{t}
of
\mathfrak{A}
, and this
x_{t}
is
\mathsf{ML\!^{i,n,u}}
-bisimilar to a set of elements in
\mathfrak{B}
. These elements of
\mathfrak{B}
can be divided into those that satisfy
r_{0}
and those that satisfy
r_{1}
. If
x_{t}
is
\mathsf{ML\!^{i,n,u}}
-bisimilar to
k
elements that satisfy
r_{0}
and
k^{\prime}
elements that satisfy
r_{1}
, then we say that registers 0 and 1 contain
\log_{2}k
and
\log_{2}k^{\prime}
, respectively, at time
t
.

An example of (part of) these intended models is shown in the following image, reproduced from Section
IV
.

\mathfrak{A},a
q_{0}
N_{c},e_{0},e_{1}
q_{1}
q_{4}
q_{4}
e_{0}
q_{2}
\vdots
R
R
R
R
\mathfrak{B},b
q_{0},r_{1}
N_{c},e_{0},e_{1}
q_{1},r_{0}
u
q_{1},r_{1}
u
q_{4},r_{0}
u
q_{4},r_{0}
q_{4},r_{1}
u
q_{4},r_{1}

q_{2},r_{1}
u
q_{4},r_{0}
e_{0},u
q_{2},r_{0}
