-Craig separation in Theorem
10
.
The
2\textsc{ExpTime}
upper bound for
\mathsf{GML\!^{i,u}}/\mathsf{ML\!^{i}}
is obtained by dropping the condition that
M_{1}
and
M_{2}
are both non-empty for mosaics
M=(M_{1},M_{2})
. The one for
\mathsf{GML\!^{i,u}}/\mathsf{ML\!^{u}}
is obtained by treating the inverse
R^{-}
with
R\in\varrho
in the same way as
R\in\sigma\setminus\varrho
. Both of these modifications give the upper bound for
\mathsf{GML\!^{i,u}}/\mathsf{ML}
.

It remains to establish
co
NExpTime
upper bounds for
\mathsf{GML\!^{i}}/\mathsf{ML\!^{i}}
and
\mathsf{GML\!^{i}}/\mathsf{ML}
.
Suppose
\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2}
are
\mathsf{GML\!^{i}}
-formulas.
We again ignore everything related to
\Diamondblack
and admit mosaics
M=(M_{1},M_{2})
with
M_{1}=\emptyset
or
M_{2}=\emptyset
(but not both). Let
m=\max\{\textit{md}(\varphi_{1}),\textit{md}(\varphi_{2})\}
. Then it follows from the proof of Lemma
16
(and the definition of
\boldsymbol{\beta}
in it) that there exist
\mathfrak{A}_{i},a_{i}
,
i=1,2
, with
\mathfrak{A}_{i}\models\varphi_{i}(a_{i})
and
\mathfrak{A}_{1},a_{1}\sim_{\mathsf{ML\!^{i}}(\varrho)}\mathfrak{A}_{2},a_{2}
iff we find words

s=M_{0}M_{1}\dots M_{n}

(9)

with
n\leq\textit{md}(\varphi,\psi)
and
M_{j}=(M_{j,1},M_{j,2})
such that

–

M_{j}
a mosaic of size
\leq k_{\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2}}^{2}
, for
j\leq n
;

–

M_{0}=M^{\ast}
for a fixed root mosaic
M^{*}=(M_{1}^{\ast},M_{2}^{\ast})
such that there are
(\mathfrak{t},\mathfrak{s}_{p},\mathfrak{s}_{c})\in M_{1}^{\ast}
and
(\mathfrak{t}^{\prime},\mathfrak{s}_{p}^{\prime},\mathfrak{s}_{c}^{\prime})\in M_{2}^{*}
with
\varphi_{1}\in\mathfrak{t}
and
\varphi_{2}\in\mathfrak{t}^{\prime}
;

–

M_{j+1}\in\mathcal{M}_{M_{j}}
, for a syntactic witness
\mathcal{M}_{M_{j}}
for
M_{j}
with
|\mathcal{M}_{M_{j}}|\leq k_{\varphi,\psi}
, for all
j<n
.

A
NExpTime
algorithm deciding non-separation could guess words of the form (
9
) and then check in exponential time that they satisfy the three conditions above.

The matching lower bounds are proved as in the previous section. This completes the proof of Theorem
10
.

\dashv

Proofs for Section
VII

We start by giving the precise recursive definition of the flattening
\mathit{fl}(\varphi)\in\mathsf{FO^{2}_{\neq}}
for
\mathsf{C^{2}}
-formulas
\varphi
without equality:

\begin{array}[]{ll}\mathit{fl}(A(x))=A(x),&\mathit{fl}(R(x,y))=R(x,y),\\
\mathit{fl}(\exists^{\geq k}x\,\varphi)=\exists x\,\mathit{fl}(\varphi),&\mathit{fl}(\neg\varphi)=\neg\mathit{fl}(\varphi),\\
\mathit{fl}(\varphi\wedge\varphi^{\prime})=\mathit{fl}(\varphi)\wedge\mathit{fl}(\varphi^{\prime}).&\end{array}

We require the following observation.

Lemma 38
.

Let
\sigma
be constant-free and let
\kappa\in\mathbb{N}^{\infty}
. Then, for all
\mathsf{FO^{2}_{\neq}}(\sigma)
-formulas
\psi_{1}(x)
and
\psi_{2}(x,y)
, all
a,b\in{\sf dom}(\mathfrak{A})
, and all
i,j,i^{\prime},j^{\prime}<\kappa
, we have
:

–

\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}\models\psi_{1}(a,i)
iff
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}\models\psi_{1}(a,j)
;

–

\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}\models\psi_{2}((a,i),(b,j))
iff
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}\models\psi_{2}((a,i^{\prime}),(b,j^{\prime}))
.

See
18

Proof.

Let
k^{\bullet}<\kappa\in\mathbb{N}^{\infty}
. First, we note that, because the signature contains no constants, for every
a\in{\sf dom}(\mathfrak{A})
and every
i<\kappa
, we have
(a,i)\in{\sf dom}(\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa})
.

We show that
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}\models\varphi(a,i)
iff
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}\models\mathit{fl}(\varphi)(a,i)
for all elements
a\in{\sf dom}(\mathfrak{A})
and
i<\kappa
. Observe that
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}\models\mathit{fl}(\varphi)(a,i)
iff
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa^{\prime}}\models\mathit{fl}(\varphi)(a,i)
follows from Lemma
17
, for all
i<\kappa
and
\kappa^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}^{\infty}
.

Now the proof proceeds by induction on the construction of
\varphi
. Assume first that
\varphi\in\mathsf{GML\!^{i,u}}
. We only discuss the critical induction step, where
\varphi
is of the form
\Diamond\!^{\geq k}_{R}\psi
for some
\psi
, in which case
\mathit{fl}(\varphi)
is of the form
\Diamond\!_{R}\,\mathit{fl}(\psi)
.

Suppose that
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}\models\Diamond\!^{\geq k}_{R}\psi(a,i)
. We assumed that
k>0
, so there is at least one
(b,j)
such that
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}\models\psi(b,j)
and
((a,i),(b,j))\in R^{\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}}
. By the induction hypothesis, we then also have
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}\models\mathit{fl}(\psi)(b,j)
, and therefore
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}\models\Diamond_{R}\,\mathit{fl}(\psi)(a,i)
.

For the other direction, suppose that
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}\models\Diamond_{R}\mathit{fl}(\psi)(a,i)
, so there is some
(b,j)
such that
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}\models\mathit{fl}(\psi)(b,j)
and
((a,i),(b,j))\in R^{\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}}
. By Lemma
38
, this implies that we have
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}\models\mathit{fl}(\psi)(b,j^{\prime})
for every
j^{\prime}<\kappa
.

By the induction hypothesis, this implies that
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}\models\psi(b,j^{\prime})
for all
j^{\prime}<\kappa
. Furthermore, by the construction of
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}
, it follows that
((a,i),(b,j^{\prime}))\in R^{\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}}
for all
j^{\prime}<\kappa
. From
k^{\bullet}<\kappa
we obtain
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}\models\Diamond\!^{\geq k}_{R}\psi(a,i)
. This completes the induction step.

The proof for
\varphi\in\mathsf{C^{2}}
is a straightforward extension of the proof above. It is again by induction on the construction of
\varphi
. For formulas
\psi(x,y)
, we claim that
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}\models\psi((a,i),(b,j))
iff
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}\models\mathit{fl}(\psi)((a,i^{\prime}),(b,j^{\prime}))
. We consider the step
\varphi=\exists^{\geq k}x\,\psi(x,y)
(we may assume that
y
is free in
\psi
). Suppose that
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}\models\exists^{\geq k}x\,\psi(a,i)
. We assumed that
k>0
, so there is at least one
(b,j)
such that
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}\models\psi((b,j),(a,i))
. By the induction hypothesis, we then also have
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}\models\mathit{fl}(\psi)((b,j),(a,i))
, and therefore
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}\models\exists x\,\mathit{fl}(\psi)(a,i)
.

For the other direction, suppose that
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}\models\exists x\,\mathit{fl}(\psi)(a,i)
, so there is some
(b,j)
such that
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}\models\mathit{fl}(\psi)((b,j),(a,i))
. By Lemma
38
, this implies that
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}\models\mathit{fl}(\psi)((b,j^{\prime}),(a,i))
for every
j^{\prime}<\kappa
.

By IH, this implies that
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}\models\psi((b,j^{\prime}),(a,i))
for all
j^{\prime}<\kappa
. From
k^{\bullet}<\kappa
we obtain
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}\models\exists^{\geq k}x\,\psi(a,i)
. This completes the induction step and thereby the proof.

\dashv

The following lemma can be shown similarly to Lemma
18
.

Lemma 39
.

Suppose
\varphi
is a
\mathsf{GML\!^{i,n,u}}(\sigma)
-formula with finite
\sigma_{c}
and
k^{\bullet}<\kappa<\kappa^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}^{\infty}
. Then

\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}\models\varphi(a,i)
iff
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}\models\mathit{fl}_{\sigma_{c}}(\varphi)(a,i)

iff
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa^{\prime}}\models\mathit{fl}_{\sigma_{c}}(\varphi)(a,i)
iff
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa^{\prime}}\models\varphi(a,i)
, for all pointed
\sigma
-structures
\mathfrak{A},a
and all
i<\kappa
.

Note that, according to the definition of
L
/
L^{\prime}
-definability, defining
L^{\prime}
-formulas
\varphi^{\prime}
are allowed to contain fresh symbols not occurring in
\varphi
. While fresh propositional variables or modal operators do not affect definability (see below), we
leave it open whether using a fresh nominal has an effect on definability of formulas without nominals.

Theorem 40
.

Let
L/L^{\prime}
be any pair of modal logics from Table
I
, with
L
admitting nominals, and let
\sigma
contain at least one constant. Then an
L(\sigma)
-formula
\varphi
is
L^{\prime}(\sigma)
-definable iff
\models\varphi\leftrightarrow\mathit{fl}_{\sigma_{c}}(\varphi)
.

Proof.

Note that if
\models\varphi\leftrightarrow\varphi^{\prime}
and
\varphi^{\prime}
uses symbols not in
\sigma
, then we can replace any variable
A\not\in\sigma
by
\top
, any
\Diamond_{R}\chi
with
R\not\in\sigma
by
\neg\top
, and any
N_{c}
with
c\not\in\sigma
by an
N_{c^{\prime}}
with
c^{\prime}\in\sigma
, and obtain
\models\varphi\leftrightarrow\varphi^{\prime\prime}
for the resulting formula
\varphi^{\prime\prime}
. Now the proof is similar to the proof of Theorem
19
.

\dashv

It remains open whether there are polynomial DAG-size formulas in
L^{\prime}

equivalent to
\mathit{fl}_{\sigma_{c}}(\Diamond\!^{\geq k}_{R}\psi)
. Note that the problem is that distinct constants can denote the same domain element.
In contrast, under the unique name assumption, polynomial DAG-size formulas
are easily constructed. Indeed, let
Th_{k}^{n}(p_{0},\dots,p_{n-1})
be a propositional formula that is true under a propositional assignment iff at least
k
variables among
p_{0},\dots,p_{n-1}
are true. It is folklore that there are such formulas of polynomial DAG-size. Let
0,1,\ldots,n-1
be the constants in
\sigma_{c}
.
Then the formula obtained by replacing in
\mathit{fl}_{\sigma_{c}}(\Diamond\!^{\geq k}_{R}\psi)
the second conjunct by the formula obtained from
Th_{k}^{n}
by replacing each
p_{i}
by
\Diamond_{R}(i\wedge\mathit{fl}_{\sigma_{c}}(\psi))
is as required.

We note that the complexity results for the validity/satisfiability problems for the logics considered in this paper do not depend on whether one defines the size of an input
\varphi
as the
formula-size
of
\varphi
(the length of
\varphi
as a word) or the
DAG-size

|\varphi|
of
\varphi
(the number of subformulas of
\varphi
).
Here, we sketch a reduction from DAG-size to formula-size by introducing
abbreviations.
Recall that
\mathit{fl}_{\sigma_{c}}(\varphi)
is equivalent to the
L
-formula
f(\varphi)
defined recursively in the same way as

\mathit{fl}_{\sigma_{c}}(\varphi)
except that

f_{\sigma_{c}}(\Diamond\!^{\geq k}_{R}\psi))=\big[\Diamond_{R}\big(\mathit{fl}_{\sigma_{c}}(\psi)\wedge\bigwedge_{c\in\sigma_{c}}\neg N_{c}\big)\vee{}\Diamond_{R}^{\geq k}(\mathit{fl}_{\sigma_{c}}(\psi)\wedge\bigvee_{c\in\sigma_{c}}N_{c})\big].

Let
\mathit{fl}^{\prime}_{\sigma_{c}}
be also defined in the same way as
\mathit{fl}_{\sigma_{c}}
except that we set, for a fresh propositional variable
A_{\psi}
,

\mathit{fl}^{\prime}_{\sigma_{c}}(\Diamond\!^{\geq k}_{R}\psi)=\big[\Diamond_{R}\big(A_{\psi}\wedge\bigwedge_{c\in\sigma_{c}}\neg N_{c}\big)\vee{}\Diamond_{R}^{\geq k}(A_{\psi}\wedge\bigvee_{c\in\sigma_{c}}N_{c})\big].

The formula-size of
\mathit{fl}^{\prime}_{\sigma_{c}}(\varphi)
is clearly polynomial in the formula-size of
\varphi
.
If
L
contains the universal modality, then
\models\varphi\leftrightarrow\mathit{fl}_{\sigma_{c}}(\varphi)
iff the following
L
-formula (of polynomial formula-size) is valid:

\blacksquare\bigwedge\{A_{\psi}\leftrightarrow\mathit{fl}^{\prime}_{\sigma_{c}}(\psi)\mid\Diamond\!^{\geq k}_{R}\psi\in\textit{sub}(\varphi)\}\rightarrow\big(\varphi\leftrightarrow\mathit{fl}^{\prime}_{\sigma_{c}}(\varphi)\big).

If
L
does not admit the universal modality, instead of
\blacksquare
we take all sequences of the form
\Box_{R_{1}}\dots\Box_{R_{m}}
such that
\varphi
contains nested
\Diamond^{\geq k_{1}}_{R_{1}}\dots\Diamond^{\geq k_{m}}_{R_{m}}
, with each
\Diamond^{\geq k_{i}}_{R_{i}}
,
i=1,\dots,m-1
, being an immediate predecessor of
\Diamond^{\geq k_{i+1}}_{R_{i+1}}
.

We now give the detailed definition of
\mathit{fl}(\varphi)
for
\mathsf{C^{2}}/\mathsf{FO^{2}_{\neq}}
. Recall that the straightforward extension of the flattening
\mathit{fl}
defined above for
\mathsf{C^{2}}
without equality does not work. So we now define a new flattening
\mathit{fl}
from
\mathsf{C^{2}}
to
\mathsf{FO^{2}_{\neq}}
. Suppose we are given a
\mathsf{C^{2}}
-formula
\varphi
.
As before, only the definition of
\mathit{fl}(\exists^{\geq k}x\,\psi)
is non-trivial. Assume that
\psi
is a Boolean combination
\beta
of the form

\psi=\beta(\rho_{1},\ldots,\rho_{k_{1}},\gamma_{1}(x),\dots,\gamma_{k_{2}}(x),\xi_{1}(y),\dots,\xi_{k_{3}}(y)),

where

–

the
\rho_{i}
are binary atoms
R(x,y)
,
R(y,x)
, or
x=y
;

–

the
\gamma_{i}(x)
are unary atoms of the form
A(x)
,
x=x
, or
R(x,x)
, or a formula of the form
\exists^{\geq k^{\prime}}y\,\gamma_{i}^{\prime}
;

–

the
\xi_{i}(y)
are unary atoms of the form
A(y)
,
y=y
or
R(y,y)
, or a formula of the form
\exists^{\geq k^{\prime}}x\,\xi_{i}^{\prime}
.

We may assume that
y
is free in
\psi
. Consider the formula

\varphi_{1}=\exists^{\geq k}x\,\big(((x=y)\wedge\psi_{1})\vee((x\not=y)\wedge\psi_{2})\big),

in which
\psi_{1}
is obtained from
\psi
by replacing

–

all
\rho_{i}
of the form
x=y
by
\top
;

–

all
\gamma_{i}(x)
of the form
x=x
by
\top
;

–

all
\xi_{i}(y)
of the form
y=y
by
\top
;

and
\psi_{2}
is obtained from
\psi
by replacing

–

all
\rho_{i}
of the form
x=y
by
\neg\top
;

–

all
\gamma_{i}(x)
of the form
x=x
by
\top
;

–

all
\xi_{i}(y)
of the form
y=y
by
\top
.

Clearly,
\varphi
and
\varphi_{1}
are logically equivalent. Also, the formula
\exists x\,((x=y)\wedge\psi_{1})

is logically equivalent to the formula
\psi_{1}^{\prime}
obtained from
\psi_{1}
by replacing all

\rho_{i}
of the form
R(x,y)
or
R(y,x)
by
R(y,y)
, all
\gamma_{i}(x)
of the form
A(x)

by
A(y)
, and all
\gamma_{i}(x)
of the form
\exists^{\geq k^{\prime}}\!y\,\gamma_{i}^{\prime}
by the result of swapping
x
and
y
. Now set, recursively,

\displaystyle\mathit{fl}(\exists^{\geq k}x\,\psi)=\mathit{fl}(\psi_{1}^{\prime})\vee\exists x\,\mathit{fl}(\psi_{2}),\quad\text{for $k=1$},

\displaystyle\mathit{fl}(\exists^{\geq k}x\,\psi)=\exists x\,\mathit{fl}(\psi_{2}),\hskip 39.83368pt\text{for $k>1$}.

Observe that we do not define
\mathit{fl}(\psi(x,y))
for formulas
\psi(x,y)
in
\mathsf{C^{2}}
with occurrences of equality that are not in the scope of a quantifier.

It is now straightforward to prove analogues of Lemma
18

and Theorem
19
for
\mathsf{C^{2}}/\mathsf{FO^{2}_{\neq}}
.

Lemma 41
.

Let
\sigma
be constant-free, let
\varphi(x)
be a
\mathsf{C^{2}}(\sigma)
-formula
(
with equality
)
, and let
k^{\bullet}<\kappa<\kappa^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}^{\infty}
. Then, for all pointed
\sigma
-structures
\mathfrak{A},a
and all
i<\kappa
, we have

\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}\models\varphi(a,i)
iff
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}\models\mathit{fl}(\varphi)(a,i)

iff
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa^{\prime}}\models\mathit{fl}(\varphi)(a,i)
iff
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa^{\prime}}\models\varphi(a,i)
.

Proof.

As before, the interesting part is to show that we have
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}\models\varphi(a,i)
iff
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}\models\mathit{fl}(\varphi)(a,i)
, for all
a\in{\sf dom}(\mathfrak{A})
and
i<\kappa
.

The proof is by induction on the construction of
\varphi
. For
\mathsf{C^{2}}
-formulas
\psi(x,y)
without occurrences of equality that are not in the scope of a quantifier, we claim that
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}\models\psi((a,i),(b,j))
iff
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}\models\mathit{fl}(\psi)((a,i^{\prime}),(b,j^{\prime}))
, for all
a,b\in{\sf dom}(\mathfrak{A})
and all
i,j,i^{\prime},j^{\prime}<\kappa
. Observe that the induction step for such formulas follows from Lemma
38
.

We consider the step
\varphi=\exists^{\geq k}x\,\psi(x,y)
, where we may assume that
y
is free in
\psi
. Assume from above:

–

\psi=\gamma(\rho_{1},\ldots,\rho_{k_{1}},\gamma_{1}(x),\ldots,\gamma_{k_{2}}(x),\xi_{1}(y),\ldots,\xi_{k_{3}}(y))
;

–

\varphi_{1}=\exists^{\geq k}x\,\big[\big((x=y)\wedge\psi_{1}\big)\vee\big((x\not=y)\wedge\psi_{2}\big)\big]
;

–

for
k=1
,

\mathit{fl}(\exists^{\geq k}x\,\psi)=\mathit{fl}(\psi_{1}^{\prime})\vee\exists x\,\mathit{fl}(\psi_{2})
;

–

for
k>1
,

\mathit{fl}(\exists^{\geq k}x\,\psi)=\exists x\,\mathit{fl}(\psi_{2}).

Suppose that
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}\models\exists^{\geq k}x\,\psi(a,i)
.

If
k=1
, then we have either
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}\models\psi((a,i),(a,i))
, and so
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}\models\psi_{1}^{\prime}(a,i)
, or
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}\models\psi((b,j),(a,i))
, for some
(b,j)\not=(a,i)
, and so
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}\models\psi_{2}((b,j),(a,i))
. Then, by IH,
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}\models\mathit{fl}(\psi_{1}^{\prime})(a,i)
or
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}\models\mathit{fl}(\psi_{2})((b,j),(a,i))
. In both cases
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}\models\mathit{fl}(\varphi)
.

If
k>1
, then
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}\models\psi((b,j),(a,i))
, for some
(b,j)\not=(a,i)
, and so again
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}\models\psi_{2}((b,j),(a,i))
and
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}\models\mathit{fl}(\varphi)
by IH.

For the other direction, suppose
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}\models\mathit{fl}(\varphi)(a,i)
.

Let
k=1
. If
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}\models\mathit{fl}(\psi_{1}^{\prime})(a,i)
, then by IH,
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}\models\psi_{1}^{\prime}(a,i)
, and so
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}\models\varphi(a,i)
. If
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}\models\exists x\,\mathit{fl}(\psi_{2})(a,i)
, then there is some
(b,j)
such that
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}\models\mathit{fl}(\psi_{2})((b,j),(a,i))
. By Lemma
38
, we have
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}\models\mathit{fl}(\psi_{2})((b,j^{\prime}),(a,i))
for some
(b,j^{\prime})\not=(a,i)
.
By the induction hypothesis, this implies that
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}\models\psi_{2}((b,j^{\prime}),(a,i))
. Thus, we obtain
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}\models\exists^{\geq k}x\,\psi(a,i)
.

Let
k>1
. Then
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}\models\exists x\,\mathit{fl}(\psi_{2}(a,i)
. We then have some
(b,j)
such that
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}\models\mathit{fl}(\psi_{2})((b,j),(a,i))
. By Lemma
38
,
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}\models\mathit{fl}(\psi_{2})((b,j^{\prime}),(a,i))
for every
j^{\prime}<\kappa
.
By the induction hypothesis, this implies that
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}\models\psi_{2}((b,j^{\prime}),(a,i))
for all
j^{\prime}<\kappa
. Since
k^{\bullet}<\kappa
, we obtain
\mathfrak{A}^{\kappa}\models\exists^{\geq k}x\,\psi(a,i)
, as required.

\dashv

For the polynomial reduction of checking
\models\varphi\leftrightarrow\mathit{fl}(\varphi)
to
\mathsf{C^{2}}
-validity,
observe that,
although
\mathit{fl}(\varphi)
is exponential in formula-size as we duplicate the formulas
\gamma_{i}(x)
and
\xi_{i}(y)
, we can again use the DAG-representation instead.

Corollary 42
.

\mathsf{C^{2}}/\mathsf{FO^{2}_{\neq}}
-definability is polytime reducible to
\mathsf{C^{2}}
-validity.

See
23

Proof.

(i)
is trivial.
(ii)
We construct
\varphi^{\prime}
by induction. The case of atomic formulas is clear. Assume
\chi(x,y)
is a Boolean combination
\beta
of the form

\chi=\beta(\gamma_{1}(x),\dots,\gamma_{k_{1}}(x),\xi_{1}(y),\dots,\xi_{k_{2}}(y)),

where

–

the
\gamma_{i}(x)
are unary atoms of the form
A(x)
,
\top(x)
, or a formula of the form
\exists y\,\gamma_{i}^{\ast}
;

–

the
\xi_{i}(y)
are unary atoms of the form
A(y)
,
\top(y)
, or a formula of the form
\exists x\,\xi_{i}^{\ast}
.

A
\gamma
-type

t
is a maximal satisfiable subset of

\{\gamma_{1}(x),\dots,\gamma_{k_{1}}(x),\neg\gamma_{1}(x),\dots,\neg\gamma_{k_{1}}(x)\}.

A
\xi
-type

t
is a maximal satisfiable subset of

\{\xi_{1}(y),\dots,\xi_{k_{2}}(y),\neg\xi_{1}(y),\dots,\neg\xi_{k_{2}}(y)\}.

Let
S
be the set of all pairs
(t_{1},t_{2})
of
\gamma
-types
t_{1}
and
\xi
-types
t_{2}
such that
t_{1}\cup t_{2}\models\chi
. Consider now, inductively, the equivalent translations
\gamma_{i}^{\prime}
and
\xi_{i}^{\prime}
of
\gamma_{i}(x)
and
\xi_{i}(y)
into
L
, respectively. For any
\gamma
-type
t
, let
t^{\prime}=\bigwedge_{\gamma\in t}\gamma^{\prime}
and, for any
\xi
-type
t
, let

t^{\prime}=\bigwedge_{\xi\in t}\xi^{\prime}
. Now define

–

(\exists y\,(R(x,y)\wedge\chi(x,y)))^{\prime}=\bigvee_{(t_{1},t_{2})\in S}(t_{1}^{\prime}\wedge\Diamond_{R}t_{2}^{\prime})
;

–

(\exists x\,(R(y,x)\wedge\chi(x,y)))^{\prime}=\bigvee_{(t_{1},t_{2})\in S}(t_{2}^{\prime}\wedge\Diamond_{R}t_{1}^{\prime})
;

–

(\exists y\,(R(y,x)\wedge\chi(x,y)))^{\prime}=\bigvee_{(t_{1},t_{2})\in S}(t_{1}^{\prime}\wedge\Diamond_{R}^{-}t_{2}^{\prime})
;

–

(\exists x\,(R(x,y)\wedge\chi(x,y)))^{\prime}=\bigvee_{(t_{1},t_{2})\in S}(t_{2}^{\prime}\wedge\Diamond_{R}^{-}t_{1}^{\prime})
;

–

(\exists y\,\chi(x,y))^{\prime}=\bigvee_{(t_{1},t_{2})\in S}(t_{1}^{\prime}\wedge\Diamondblack t_{2}^{\prime})
;

–

(\exists x\,\chi(x,y))^{\prime}=\bigvee_{(t_{1},t_{2})\in S}(t_{2}^{\prime}\wedge\Diamondblack t_{1}^{\prime})
.

It is straightforward to see that
\cdot^{\prime}
is a map from suc
L
to
L
and that

\mathfrak{A}\models\varphi(a)
iff
\mathfrak{A}\models\varphi^{\prime}(a)
, for all pointed
\mathfrak{A},a
.

\dashv

We now consider
\mathsf{FO^{2}_{\neq}}/\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}
-definability, where
\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}
is represented in suc
\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}
. For every
\mathfrak{A}
, we take the
twin-unfolding

\mathfrak{A}^{\ast}
of
\mathfrak{A}
whose domain
{\sf dom}(\mathfrak{A}^{\ast})
is the set of

\vec{a}=a_{0}^{j}S_{0}\dots S_{n-1}a_{n}^{j}

(10)

with
n\geq 0
,
j\in\{0,1\}
,
a_{i}\in{\sf dom}(\mathfrak{A})
for all
i\leq n
, and
S_{i}
either a binary predicate symbol in
\mathit{sig}(\varphi)
or its inverse, and such that
(a_{i},a_{i+1})\in S_{i}^{\mathfrak{A}}
for
i\leq n
. For
\vec{a}
of the form (
10
), let
\text{tail}(\vec{a})=a_{n}^{j}
. Set
\vec{a}\in A^{\mathfrak{A}^{\ast}}
if
a_{n}\in A^{\mathfrak{A}}
for

\text{tail}(\vec{a})=a_{n}^{j}
.
It remains to define
R^{\mathfrak{A}^{\ast}}
for a binary predicate symbol
R
. Assume
\vec{a}
takes the form (
10
) and
let
\vec{a}^{\prime}=a_{0}^{j}S_{0}\cdots a_{n}^{j}S_{n}a_{n+1}^{j}
be an extension of
\vec{a}
. Then
(\vec{a},\vec{a}^{\prime})\in R^{\mathfrak{A}^{\ast}}
if
R=S_{n}
and

(\vec{a}^{\prime},\vec{a})\in R^{\mathfrak{A}^{\ast}}
if
R^{-}=S_{n}
.

Observe that
\mathfrak{A},a\sim_{\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}}\mathfrak{A}^{\ast},a^{j}
for
j=0,1
since
\vec{a}\mapsto\text{tail}(\vec{a})
is an
\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}
-bisimulation between
\mathfrak{A}^{\ast}
and
\mathfrak{A}
.

Lemma 43
.

For all
\varphi\in\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}
and all pointed
\mathfrak{A},a
:
\mathfrak{A}^{\ast}\models\varphi(a^{0})
iff
\mathfrak{A}\models\varphi(a)
.

Lemma 44
.

For all pointed structures
\mathfrak{A},a
and
j=0,1
, we have
\mathfrak{A}^{\ast}\models\varphi(a^{j})
iff
\mathfrak{A}^{\ast}\models m(\varphi)(a^{j})
.

Proof.

The proof uses the following properties of
\mathfrak{A}^{\ast}
: (1) for all
a,b\in{\sf dom}(\mathfrak{A}^{\ast})
,
if
(a,b)\in R^{\mathfrak{A}^{\ast}}
, then
(a,b)\not\in S^{\mathfrak{A}^{\ast}}
for any
S\not=R
and
(b,a)\not\in S^{\mathfrak{A}^{\ast}}
for any
S
; (2) for all
a\in{\sf dom}(\mathfrak{A}^{\ast}
and all Boolean combination of subformulas
\psi
of
m(\varphi)
with one free variable, if
\mathfrak{A}^{\ast}\models\psi(a)
, then there exists
b\in{\sf dom}(\mathfrak{A}^{\ast})
such that
\mathfrak{A}^{\ast}\models\psi(b)
,
b\not=a
, and
(a,b),(b,a)\not\in S^{\mathfrak{A}^{\ast}}
for any
S\in\mathit{sig}(\varphi)
.

\dashv

We are now in a position to show that
m(\varphi)
is a suc
\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}
-formula defining
\varphi
whenever such a definition exists.

Theorem 45
.

An
\mathsf{FO^{2}_{\neq}}
-formula
\varphi(x)
is suc
\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}
-definable iff
\models\varphi\leftrightarrow m(\varphi)
.

Proof.

Follows from Lemmas
43
and
44
.

\dashv

As
m(\varphi)
is constructed in polytime in DAG-representation, we obtain:

Corollary 46
.

\mathsf{C^{2}}/\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}
-definability is polytime reducible to
\mathsf{C^{2}}
-validity.

We next consider the elimination of the universal and inverse modalities.

Define the
\omega
-unfolding

\mathfrak{A}^{\updownarrow\omega}
of
a structure
\mathfrak{A}
as follows. Its domain
{\sf dom}(\mathfrak{A}^{\updownarrow\omega})
is the set of
all

\vec{a}=a_{0}^{j_{0}}S_{0}\cdots S_{n-1}a_{n}^{j_{n}},

(11)

where
n\geq 0
,
j_{i}\in\omega
,
a_{i}\in{\sf dom}(\mathfrak{A})
for all
i\leq n
, and
S_{i}
is either a binary predicate symbol in
\mathit{sig}(\varphi)
or its inverse such that
(a_{i},a_{i+1})\in S_{i}^{\mathfrak{A}}
for
i\leq n
. Let
\text{tail}(\vec{a})=a_{n}^{j}
.
For
\vec{a}
of the form (
11
), we set
\vec{a}\in A^{\mathfrak{A}^{\updownarrow\omega}}
iff
a_{n}\in A^{\mathfrak{A}}
. Let
\vec{a}
be of the form (
11
) and let
\vec{a}^{\prime}=a_{0}^{j_{0}}S_{0}\cdots a_{n}^{j_{n}}S_{n}a_{n+1}^{j_{n+1}}
be an extension of
\vec{a}
. Then
(\vec{a},\vec{a}^{\prime})\in R^{\mathfrak{A}^{\updownarrow\omega}}
iff
R=S_{n}
and

(\vec{a}^{\prime},\vec{a})\in R^{\mathfrak{A}^{\updownarrow\omega}}
iff
R^{-}=S_{n}
.

Lemma 47
.

For all pointed structures
\mathfrak{A},a
, we have
\mathfrak{A}^{\updownarrow\omega},a^{0}\sim_{\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}}\mathfrak{A},a
.

\mathfrak{A}_{0}
is a
generated substructure
of
\mathfrak{A}_{1}
if it is a substructure of
\mathfrak{A}_{1}
and
a\in\text{dom}(\mathfrak{A}_{0})
and
(a,a^{\prime})\in R^{\mathfrak{A}_{1}}
or

(a^{\prime},a)\in R^{\mathfrak{A}_{1}}
imply
a^{\prime}\in{\sf dom}(\mathfrak{A}_{0})
.

\mathfrak{A}_{0}
is a
forward generated substructure
of
\mathfrak{A}_{1}
if it is a substructure of
\mathfrak{A}_{1}
and
a\in\text{dom}(\mathfrak{A}_{0})
and
(a,a^{\prime})\in R^{\mathfrak{A}_{1}}
imply
a^{\prime}\in{\sf dom}(\mathfrak{A}_{0})
. By
\mathfrak{A}_{a}
we denote the smallest
generated substructure of
\mathfrak{A}
containing
a\in{\sf dom}(\mathfrak{A})
.

Define the
forward
\omega
-unfolding

\mathfrak{A}^{\uparrow\omega}
as the
substructure of
\mathfrak{A}^{\updownarrow\omega}
, in which
{\sf dom}(\mathfrak{A}^{\uparrow\omega})
is the set of all

\vec{a}=a_{0}^{j_{0}}R_{0}\cdots R_{n-1}a_{n}^{j_{n}},

where each
R_{i}
is a binary predicate symbol in
\mathit{sig}(\varphi)
. Forward
\omega
-unfoldings have been considered before in the context of
\mathsf{ML}
-definability
[
31
,
32
]
.
Note that
\mathfrak{A}^{\uparrow\omega}
is a forward generated substructure of
\mathfrak{A}^{\updownarrow\omega}
and we have the following:

Lemma 48
.

\mathfrak{A}^{\uparrow\omega},a^{0}\sim_{\mathsf{ML\!^{u}}}\mathfrak{A},a
.

For pointed structures
\mathfrak{A},a
and
\mathfrak{B},b
, we write
\mathfrak{A},a\cong\mathfrak{B},b
if there is an isomorphism from
\mathfrak{A}
onto
\mathfrak{B}
mapping
a
to
b
.

Lemma 49
.

For any at most countable structures
\mathfrak{A},\mathfrak{B}
with

a\in{\sf dom}(\mathfrak{A})
and
b\in{\sf dom}(\mathfrak{B})
,

–

if
\mathfrak{A},a\sim_{\mathsf{ML\!^{i}}}\mathfrak{B},b
, then

\mathfrak{A}_{a^{0}}^{\updownarrow\omega},a^{0}\cong\mathfrak{B}_{b^{0}}^{\updownarrow\omega},b^{0}
;

–

if
\mathfrak{A},a\sim_{\mathsf{ML}}\mathfrak{B},b
, then

\mathfrak{A}_{a^{0}}^{\uparrow\omega},a^{0}\cong\mathfrak{B}_{b^{0}}^{\uparrow\omega},b^{0}
.

We say that
\varphi
is
invariant under
(
forward
)
generated substructures
if, for all pointed
\mathfrak{A},a
and all (forward) generated substructures
\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}
of
\mathfrak{A}
with
a\in{\sf dom}(\mathfrak{A}^{\prime})
, we have
\mathfrak{A}\models\varphi(a)
iff
\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}\models\varphi(a)
.

Lemma 50
.

The following hold
:

1.

\varphi\in\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}
is
\mathsf{ML\!^{i}}
-definable iff
\varphi
is invariant under generated substructures
;

2.

\varphi\in\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}
is
\mathsf{ML}
-definable iff
\varphi
is invariant under forward generated substructures.

Proof.

(1) It is straightforward to show that every formula in
\mathsf{ML\!^{i}}
is invariant under generated substructures. Hence the direction from left to right follows. Conversely, assume that
\varphi\in\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}
is not
\mathsf{ML\!^{i}}
-definable but is invariant under generated substructures. We find countable
\mathfrak{A},a
and
\mathfrak{B},b
with
\mathfrak{A}\models\varphi(a)
,
\mathfrak{B}\models\neg\varphi(b)
, and
\mathfrak{A},a\sim_{\mathsf{ML\!^{i}}}\mathfrak{B},b
.
By Lemma
49
, we have

\mathfrak{A}_{a^{0}}^{\updownarrow\omega},a^{0}\cong\mathfrak{A}_{b^{0}}^{\updownarrow\omega},b^{0}
.
By invariance of
\varphi
under generated substructures,
\mathfrak{A}_{a^{0}}^{\updownarrow\omega}\models\varphi(a^{0})
because

\mathfrak{A}_{a^{0}}^{\updownarrow\omega}
is a generated substructure of

\mathfrak{A}^{\updownarrow\omega}
and
\mathfrak{A}^{\updownarrow\omega}\models\varphi(a^{0})

because, by Lemma
47
,
\mathfrak{A}^{\updownarrow\omega},a^{0}\sim_{\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}}\mathfrak{A},a
.

By invariance of
\varphi
under generated substructures, we obtain
\mathfrak{B}_{b^{0}}^{\updownarrow\omega}\models\neg\varphi(b^{0})
because

\mathfrak{B}_{b^{0}}^{\updownarrow\omega}
is a generated substructure of

\mathfrak{B}^{\updownarrow\omega}
and
\mathfrak{B}^{\updownarrow\omega}\models\neg\varphi(b^{0})

because, by Lemma
47
,
\mathfrak{B}^{\updownarrow\omega},b^{0}\sim_{\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}}\mathfrak{B},b
.

We have derived a contradiction as isomorphic pointed structures satisfy the same formulas.

(2) It is straightforward to show that every formula in
\mathsf{ML}
is invariant under forward generated substructures. Hence the direction from left to right follows. Conversely, assume that
\varphi\in\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}
is not
\mathsf{ML}
-definable but is invariant under forward generated substructures. We find countable
\mathfrak{A},a
and
\mathfrak{B},b
with
\mathfrak{A}\models\varphi(a)
,
\mathfrak{B}\models\neg\varphi(b)
, and
\mathfrak{A},a\sim_{\mathsf{ML}}\mathfrak{B},b
.
By Lemma
49
,

\mathfrak{A}_{a^{0}}^{\uparrow\omega},a^{0}\cong\mathfrak{A}_{b^{0}}^{\uparrow\omega},b^{0}
.
By invariance of
\varphi
under forward generated substructures,
\mathfrak{A}_{a^{0}}^{\uparrow\omega}\models\varphi(a^{0})
because

\mathfrak{A}_{a^{0}}^{\uparrow\omega}
is a forward generated substructure of

\mathfrak{A}^{\updownarrow\omega}
and
\mathfrak{A}^{\updownarrow\omega}\models\varphi(a^{0})

because, by Lemma
47
,
\mathfrak{A}^{\updownarrow\omega},a^{0}\sim_{\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}}\mathfrak{A},a
.

By invariance of
\varphi
under forward generated substructures,
\mathfrak{B}_{b^{0}}^{\uparrow\omega}\models\neg\varphi(b^{0})
because

\mathfrak{B}_{b^{0}}^{\uparrow\omega}
is a forward generated substructure of

\mathfrak{B}^{\updownarrow\omega}
and
\mathfrak{B}^{\updownarrow\omega}\models\neg\varphi(b^{0})

because, by Lemma
47
,
\mathfrak{B}^{\updownarrow\omega},b^{0}\sim_{\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}}\mathfrak{B},b
.

We have derived a contradiction as isomorphic pointed structures satisfy the same formulas.

\dashv

Theorem 51
.

Both
\mathsf{C^{2}}/\mathsf{ML\!^{i}}
- and
\mathsf{C^{2}}/\mathsf{ML}
-definability are
coNExpTime
-complete.

Proof.

We consider
\mathsf{C^{2}}/\mathsf{ML}
-definability, the proof for
\mathsf{C^{2}}/\mathsf{ML\!^{i}}
-definability is similar. Observe that we can encode the problem of deciding whether a formula
\varphi(x)
in suc
\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}
is invariant under forward generated substructures as a validity problem in suc
\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}
. To show this, let
A
be a fresh unary predicate symbol and let
\varphi(x)_{|A}
be the relativisation of
\varphi(x)
to
A
. Observe that, for all pointed
\mathfrak{A},a
, we have
\mathfrak{A}\models\varphi_{|A}(a)
iff
\mathfrak{A}_{|A}\models\varphi(a)
, where
\mathfrak{A}_{|A}
is the substructure of
\mathfrak{A}
induced by
A^{\mathfrak{A}}

and
a\in A^{\mathfrak{A}}
. Then
\varphi(x)
is invariant under forward generated substructures iff,
for

\chi_{A}=\bigwedge_{R\in\mathit{sig}(\varphi)}\forall x(A(x)\rightarrow\forall y(R(x,y)\rightarrow A(y))),

the formula

\chi_{A}\rightarrow\forall x((\varphi(x)\wedge A(x))\leftrightarrow\varphi(x)_{|A})

is valid. Hence
\varphi(x)\in\mathsf{C^{2}}
is not
\mathsf{ML}
-definable iff at least one of the following conditions holds:

1.

\varphi\leftrightarrow\mathit{fl}(\varphi)
is not valid;

2.

\mathit{fl}(\varphi)\leftrightarrow m(\mathit{fl}(\varphi))
is not valid;

3.

m(\mathit{fl}(\varphi))
is not invariant under forward generated substructures.

The claim follows since each of these conditions can be checked in
NExpTime
.

\dashv

Proofs for Section
VIII

See
26

Proof.

We first complete the proof of
(a)\Rightarrow(d)
for the remaining pairs
L/L^{\prime}
of modal logics. If the universal modality is not admitted in
L^{\prime}
, then we take the set
\Gamma^{\prime}
of all
L^{\prime}_{2\textit{md}(\varphi)+2}(\varrho)
-formulas
\chi
with
\mathfrak{B}^{k^{\bullet}+1}\models\chi(a,0)

and
\gamma^{\prime}=\bigwedge_{\chi\in\Gamma^{\prime}}\chi
. Then, for every sequence
\Box=\Box_{R_{1}}\cdots\Box_{R_{n}}
with
n\leq\textit{md}(\varphi)
and
R_{1},\dots,R_{n}\in\varrho
, every
\chi\in\textit{sub}(\mathit{fl}(\varphi))
, and every
R\in\varrho
,
\Gamma
contains

\Box\Big(\Diamond_{R}\chi\rightarrow\big(\Diamond_{R}(\chi\wedge A_{0})\wedge\cdots\wedge\Diamond_{R}(\chi\wedge A_{k^{\bullet}+1})\big)\Big)

and, for every sequence
\Box=\Box_{R_{1}}\cdots\Box_{R_{n}}
with
n\leq 2\textit{md}(\varphi)
and
R_{1},\dots,R_{n}\in\varrho
, it contains the formula

\Box(A_{i}\rightarrow\neg A_{j}),\quad\text{for $0\leq i<j\leq k^{\bullet}$}.

This again gives
\gamma^{\prime}\models\varphi\leftrightarrow\mathit{fl}(\varphi)
.
If
L^{\prime}
admits inverse modalities, in the formulas
\Gamma^{\prime}
above we have to consider not only
R\in\varrho
but also
R^{-}
with
R\in\varrho
. This allows us to show again that

\gamma^{\prime}\models\varphi\leftrightarrow\mathit{fl}(\varphi)
.

The implication
(b)\Rightarrow(a)
is trivial.

(c)\Rightarrow(b)
Let
\varphi\models\mathit{fl}(\varphi)
. To prove that
\mathit{fl}(\varphi)
is a uniform
L^{\prime}(\sigma)
-separator for
\varphi
, suppose
\varphi\models\chi
, for an
L^{\prime}(\sigma)
-formula

, aiming to show
\mathit{fl}(\varphi)\models\chi
. Let
\mathfrak{A}\models\mathit{fl}(\varphi)(a)

\mathfrak{A}^{\omega}\models\mathit{fl}(\varphi)(a)
, by Lemma
17
. Then
\mathfrak{A}^{\omega}\models\varphi(a)

by Lemma
18
, and so
\mathfrak{A}^{\omega}\models\chi(a)
. Using again Lemma
17
, we obtain
\mathfrak{A}\models\chi(a)
, as required.

(d)\Rightarrow(c)
Suppose
\mathfrak{A}\models\varphi(a)
. As
\varphi
is preserved under
\omega
-expansions,
\mathfrak{A}^{\omega}\models\varphi(a)
. Hence
\mathfrak{A}^{\omega}\models\mathit{fl}(\varphi)(a)
, by Lemma

. By Lemma

,
\mathfrak{A}\models\mathit{fl}(\varphi)(a)
, as required.

The proof for
\mathsf{C^{2}}/\mathsf{FO^{2}_{\neq}}
is similar and left to the reader.

See
29

Proof.

We first give the construction of
\text{diag}_{\sigma_{f}}(\mathfrak{A},a)
for the pair
\mathsf{GML\!^{i,n,u}}/\mathsf{ML\!^{i,n,u}}
. Consider a finite pointed
\sigma_{f}
-structure
\mathfrak{A},a
, where
\mathit{sig}(\varphi)\subseteq\sigma_{f}\subseteq\sigma
and
\sigma_{f}
is finite. Identify the elements of
{\sf dom}(\mathfrak{A})
with some constants in
\sigma\setminus\sigma_{f}
. Then define
\text{diag}_{\sigma_{f}}(\mathfrak{A},a)
as the conjunction of the following formulas:

–

N_{a}
,

–

\blacksquare\bigvee_{c\in{\sf dom}(\mathfrak{A})}N_{c}
,

–

\Diamondblack N_{c}
, for
c\in{\sf dom}(\mathfrak{A})
,

–

\blacksquare(N_{c}\rightarrow\neg N_{c^{\prime}})
, for distinct
c,c^{\prime}\in{\sf dom}(\mathfrak{A})
,

–

\Diamondblack(N_{c}\wedge A)
, for
c\in A^{\mathfrak{A}}
,

–

\Diamondblack(N_{c}\wedge N_{d})
, for
c=d^{\mathfrak{A}}
and
d\in\sigma_{f}
,

–

\Diamondblack(N_{c}\wedge\neg A)
, for
c\not\in A^{\mathfrak{A}}
,

–

\Diamondblack(N_{c}\wedge\Diamond_{R}N_{d})
, for
(c,d)\in R^{\mathfrak{A}}
,

–

\Diamondblack(N_{c}\wedge\neg\Diamond_{R}N_{d})
, for
(c,d)\not\in R^{\mathfrak{A}}
.

It is easy to see that
\text{diag}_{\sigma_{f}}(\mathfrak{A},a)
has the properties (i) and (ii) from the proof sketch in the main paper.

We now show the directions
(a)\Leftrightarrow(b)
and
(c)\Rightarrow(b)
for all pairs
L/L^{\prime}
from the theorem.

(a)\Rightarrow(b)
Consider a uniform
L^{\prime}(\sigma)
-separator
\varphi^{\prime}
for
\varphi
.
Clearly,
\varphi^{\prime}
is a uniform
L^{\prime}(\sigma^{\prime})
-separator for
\varphi
, for any signature
\sigma^{\prime}
obtained from
\sigma
by removing all but a finite set
\sigma_{0}^{\prime}
of constants that contains all constants occurring in
\varphi
. By Theorem
27
,
\mathit{fl}_{\sigma_{0}^{\prime}}(\varphi)
is also a uniform
L(\sigma^{\prime})
-separator for
\varphi
. By the definition of uniform separators, we have:

–

\varphi^{\prime}\models\mathit{fl}_{\sigma_{0}^{\prime}}(\varphi)
for any such
\sigma_{0}^{\prime}
;

–

\mathit{fl}_{\sigma_{0}^{\prime}}(\varphi)\models\varphi^{\prime}
for any such
\sigma_{0}^{\prime}
with
\sigma_{0}^{\prime}\supseteq\sigma_{0}
.

It follows that
\models\varphi^{\prime}\leftrightarrow\mathit{fl}_{\sigma_{0}}(\varphi)
, as required.

(b)\Rightarrow(a)
is trivial.

(c)\Rightarrow(b)
Suppose
\varphi\models\chi
, for an
L^{\prime}(\sigma)
-formula
\chi
. We have to show that
\mathit{fl}_{\sigma_{0}}(\varphi)\models\chi
. Assume this is not the case. By the FMP of
L^{\prime}
, we have a finite pointed structure
\mathfrak{A},a
with
\mathfrak{A}\models\mathit{fl}_{\sigma_{0}}(\varphi)(a)
and
\mathfrak{A}\not\models\chi(a)
. But then, in view of
\mathit{fl}_{\sigma_{0}}(\varphi)\models_{\it fin}\varphi
and
\varphi\models\chi
, we have
\mathfrak{A}\models\chi(a)
, which is impossible.

It remains to show
(b)\Rightarrow(c)
for the remaining pairs
L/L^{\prime}
.
For
\mathsf{GML\!^{n,u}}/\mathsf{ML\!^{n,u}}
, the proof is the same as in the main part of the paper since
\text{diag}_{\sigma_{f}}(\mathfrak{A},a)
is in
\mathsf{ML\!^{n,u}}
.

For
\mathsf{GML\!^{i,n}}/\mathsf{ML\!^{i,n}}
and
\mathsf{GML\!^{n}}/\mathsf{ML\!^{n}}
, we construct
\text{diag}_{\sigma_{f}}(\mathfrak{A},a)
without using
\Diamondblack
.
We still require condition (ii) but only the following weaker versions of condition (i):

(i)

for
\mathsf{GML\!^{i,n}}
: if
\mathfrak{B}\models\text{diag}_{\sigma_{f}}(\mathfrak{A},a)(b)
, then
\mathfrak{A}_{a},a\cong_{\sigma_{f}}\mathfrak{B}_{b},b
for the smallest generated substructures
\mathfrak{A}_{a}
and
\mathfrak{B}_{b}
of
\mathfrak{A}
and
\mathfrak{B}
containing

a
and
b
, respectively;

(i)

for
\mathsf{GML\!^{n}}
: if
\mathfrak{B}\models\text{diag}_{\sigma_{f}}(\mathfrak{A},a)(b)
, then
\mathfrak{A}_{a},a\cong_{\sigma_{f}}\mathfrak{B}_{b},b
for the smallest forward generated substructures
\mathfrak{A}_{a}
and
\mathfrak{B}_{b}
of
\mathfrak{A}
and
\mathfrak{B}
containing

a
and
b
, respectively.

Recall from the previous section that
then the domain of
\mathfrak{A}_{a}
comprises all
a^{\prime}\in{\sf dom}(\mathfrak{A})
such that
a^{\prime}=a
or
a^{\prime}
is reachable from
a
via a path

a=a_{0}S_{1}a_{1}S_{2}\dots S_{n}a_{n}=a^{\prime},

where all
a_{i}
are in
{\sf dom}(\mathfrak{A})
and all
S_{i}
are binary relations from
\sigma_{f}
(for
\mathsf{ML\!^{n}}
) and inverses thereof (for
\mathsf{ML\!^{i,n}}
).
For each
a^{\prime}\in{\sf dom}(\mathfrak{A})
,
a^{\prime}\neq a
, we fix a shortest path of this form and set

\Diamond_{a^{\prime}}=\Diamond_{S_{1}}\dots\Diamond_{S_{n}}
,
\Box_{a^{\prime}}=\Box_{S_{1}}\dots\Box_{S_{n}}
,
regarding both
\Diamond_{a}
and
\Box_{a}
as blank. We can now replace the conjuncts of the form
\Diamondblack\psi
and
\blacksquare\psi
in
\text{diag}_{\sigma_{f}}(\mathfrak{A},a)
by

\bigvee_{c\in{\sf dom}(\mathfrak{A}_{a})}\hskip-5.69054pt\Diamond_{c}\psi\quad\text{and}\quad\bigwedge_{c\in{\sf dom}(\mathfrak{A}_{a})}\hskip-5.69054pt\Box_{c}\psi,

respectively, and add the conjuncts

–

\Box_{c}(N_{c}\to\Box_{S}\hskip-5.69054pt\displaystyle\bigvee_{(c,d)\in S^{\mathfrak{A}}}\hskip-5.69054ptN_{d})
, for every relevant binary relation
S
.

Denote the resulting
L^{\prime}(\sigma_{f})
-formulas by
\text{diag}_{\sigma_{f}}^{L^{\prime}}(\mathfrak{A},a)
, where
L^{\prime}\in\{\mathsf{ML\!^{i,n}},\mathsf{ML\!^{n}}\}
. It is readily checked that they satisfy the respective versions of (i) for
L^{\prime}
.

It is now straightforward to lift the proof of
(b)\Rightarrow(c)
to the pairs
\mathsf{GML\!^{i,n}}/\mathsf{ML\!^{i,n}}
and
\mathsf{GML\!^{n}}/\mathsf{ML\!^{n}}
.

\dashv

It follows that, for the pairs
L/L^{\prime}
considered in Theorem
29
, uniform
L^{\prime}(\sigma)
-separator existence for
\sigma
containing infinitely many constants reduces in polytime to
finite and general
L
-valdity. For logics with the FMP, it follows from Theorems
29
and
40
that uniform separator existence and definability coincide:

Theorem 52
.

Let
L/L^{\prime}
be any of the pairs
\mathsf{GML\!^{n,u}}/\mathsf{ML\!^{n,u}}
,
\mathsf{GML\!^{u}}/\mathsf{ML\!^{u}}
,
\mathsf{GML\!^{n}}/\mathsf{ML\!^{n}}
, or
\mathsf{GML}/\mathsf{ML}
. Then, for any signature
\sigma
with infinite
\sigma_{c}
and any
L(\sigma)
-formula
\varphi
, a uniform
L^{\prime}(\sigma)
-separator for
\varphi
is an
L^{\prime}
-definition of
\varphi
.

We consider uniform
\mathsf{FO^{2}_{\neq}}/\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}
-separation.

Theorem 53
.

For any signature
\sigma
with
\sigma_{c}=\emptyset
and any
\mathsf{FO^{2}_{\neq}}(\sigma)
-formula
\varphi
, the following conditions are equivalent
:

(a)

\varphi
has a uniform
\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}(\sigma)
-separator
;

(b)

m(\varphi)
is a uniform
\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}(\sigma)
-separator for
\varphi
;

(c)

\varphi\models m(\varphi)
;

(d)

\varphi
is preserved under twin-unfoldings of
\sigma
-structures
\mathfrak{A}
in the sense that
\mathfrak{A}\models\varphi(a)
implies
\mathfrak{A}^{\ast}\models\varphi(a^{0})
, for any
\mathfrak{A},a
.

Proof.

(a)\Rightarrow(d)
. Given a uniform
\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}(\sigma)
-separator
\varphi^{\prime}
for
\varphi
, we need to show that
\varphi
is preserved under twin unfoldings of
\sigma
-structures. Suppose this is not so. Take a pointed
\sigma
-structure
\mathfrak{A},a
such that
\mathfrak{A}\models\varphi(a)
and
\mathfrak{A}^{\ast}\not\models\varphi(a^{0})
.
From (a) we obtain
\mathfrak{A}\models\varphi^{\prime}(a)
, and so, by By Lemma
43
,
\mathfrak{A}^{\ast}\models\varphi^{\prime}(a^{0})
.

For any signature
\varrho
and
k\geq 0
, let suc
\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}_{k}(\varrho)
be the set of all suc
\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}(\varrho)
-formulas of rank
\leq k
. If
\varrho
is finite, then suc
\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}(\varrho)
is finite modulo logical equivalence.

The proof is now similar to the proof of Theorem
26
. We construct a formula
\gamma(x)\in\text{suc}\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}
using fresh unary relation symbols such that
\mathfrak{A}^{\ast}
can always be expanded to a model of
\gamma
and
\gamma\models\varphi\leftrightarrow m(\varphi)
. The latter is achieved by constructing
\gamma

in such a way that it ensures conditions (1) and (2) of the proof of Lemma
44
.

Consider fresh unary
A_{0}^{S},A_{1}^{S},A_{2}^{S},B_{0},B_{1}
, for

S\in\mathcal{R}(\varphi)
with
\mathcal{R}(\varphi)=\{R\mid R\in\mathit{sig}(\varphi)\}\cup\{R^{-}\mid R\in\mathit{sig}(\varphi)\}
. Let
\varrho
be the union of the set of fresh symbols and
\mathit{sig}(\varphi)\cup\mathit{sig}(\varphi^{\prime})
. We first ensure condition 2 by stating that
B_{0},B_{1}
partition the domain, satisfy the same formulas in suc
\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}_{k}(\varrho)
, and that elements satisfying
B_{0}
and
B_{1}
are not related by any
R\in\sigma
. So let
\gamma
contain the conjuncts

\displaystyle\blacksquare((B_{0}\rightarrow\neg B_{1})\wedge(B_{0}\vee B_{1})),

\displaystyle\blacksquare((B_{0}\rightarrow\neg\Diamond_{R}B_{1})\wedge(B_{1}\rightarrow\neg\Diamond_{R}B_{0})),

\displaystyle\Diamondblack(\chi\wedge B_{0})\leftrightarrow\Diamondblack(\chi\wedge B_{1}),

for all
\chi\in\text{suc}\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}_{k}(\varrho)
. To enforce condition 1,
\gamma
states that the
A_{0}^{S},A_{1}^{S},A_{2}^{S}
partition the domain:

\displaystyle\blacksquare\bigvee_{0\leq i\leq 2,S\in\mathcal{R}(\varphi)}A_{i}^{S},

\displaystyle\blacksquare\bigwedge_{A_{i}^{S}\not=A_{j}^{S^{\prime}}}(A_{I}^{S}\rightarrow\neg A_{j}^{S^{\prime}}),

and that the relations
S
can link the
A_{i}^{S^{\prime}}
only in a certain way: for all
S,S^{\prime},S^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{R}(\varphi)
and all
i\leq 2
:

\displaystyle\blacksquare(A_{i}^{S}\rightarrow\Box_{S^{\prime}}\neg A_{i\oplus_{3}1}^{S^{\prime\prime}}),\text{ if $S^{\prime}\not=S^{\prime\prime}$},

\displaystyle\blacksquare(A_{i}^{S}\rightarrow\Box_{S^{\prime}}\neg A_{i}^{S^{\prime\prime}}).

Then
\gamma
is satisfied in any
\varrho
-structure
\mathfrak{B}^{\ast}
obtained from some
\mathfrak{A}^{\ast}
by satisfying
B_{0}
in all nodes of the form (
10
) with
j=0
,
B_{1}
in all nodes of the form (
10
) with
j=1
and by satisfying
A_{i}^{S}
in a node of the form (
10
) if there is
k\geq 0
with
n=3k+i
and
S_{n-1}=S
. Then
\mathfrak{B}^{\ast}\models\gamma(a^{0})
.
By construction,
\gamma\models\varphi\leftrightarrow m(\varphi)
.
As a consequence, we have
\varphi\models\gamma\rightarrow m(\varphi)
, and so, by the definition of uniform
\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}(\sigma)
-separators,
\varphi^{\prime}\models\gamma\rightarrow m(\varphi)
. Since
\mathfrak{B}^{\ast}\models\varphi^{\prime}(a^{0})
and
\mathfrak{B}^{\ast}\models\gamma(a^{0})
, we obtain
\mathfrak{B}^{\ast}\models m(\varphi)(a^{0})
. Therefore,
\mathfrak{A}^{\ast}\models m(\varphi)(a^{0})
as
m(\varphi)
does not contain any fresh symbols. It follows from Lemma
43
that
\mathfrak{A}^{\ast}\models\varphi(a^{0})
, which is impossible.

The implication
(b)\Rightarrow(a)
is trivial.

(c)\Rightarrow(b)
Let
\varphi\models m(\varphi)
. To prove that
m(\varphi)
is a uniform
\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}(\sigma)
-separator for
\varphi
, suppose
\varphi\models\chi
, for an
\mathsf{ML\!^{i,u}}(\sigma)
-formula
\chi
, aiming to show
m(\varphi)\models\chi
. Let
\mathfrak{A}\models m(\varphi)(a)
. Then
\mathfrak{A}^{\ast}\models m(\varphi)(a)
, by Lemma
44
. Then
\mathfrak{A}^{\ast}\models\varphi(a)

by Lemma
43
, and so
\mathfrak{A}^{\ast}\models\chi(a)
. Using again Lemma
44
, we obtain
\mathfrak{A}\models\chi(a)
, as required.

(d)\Rightarrow(c)
Suppose
\mathfrak{A}\models\varphi(a)
. As
\varphi
is preserved under twin-unfoldings,
\mathfrak{A}^{\ast}\models\varphi(a^{0})
. Hence
\mathfrak{A}^{\ast}\models m(\varphi)(a^{0})
, by Lemma
43
. By Lemma
44
,
\mathfrak{A}\models m(\varphi)(a)
, as required.

\dashv
